===== Animal2AnyOf4.php =====
<?php

declare(strict_types=1);

namespace GoldenCorpus;

use Closure;
use Illuminate\Contracts\Validation\ValidationRule;
use Illuminate\Contracts\Validation\ValidatorAwareRule;
use Illuminate\Validation\Validator;
use LogicException;
use Spatie\LaravelData\Data;

use function is_string;
use function trim;

/**
 * NOTE: This DTO is auto-generated from an OpenAPI schema.
 * Do not edit this file manually.
 *
 * laravel-data mode: one class, hydrated and validated by `spatie/laravel-data`.
 *
 *     $dto = Animal2AnyOf4::from($request);
 *
 * Validation runs on that call because `validation_strategy` defaults to `OnlyRequests`. Given a plain
 * array instead, use `Animal2AnyOf4::validateAndCreate($payload)` — `from($array)` hydrates WITHOUT
 * validating.
 */
final class Animal2AnyOf4 extends Data implements Animal2
{
    public function __construct(
        public readonly string $test2,
    ) {
    }

    /**
     * Validation rules for the framework's validator, keyed by the OpenAPI property names — which is
     * what the request payload carries, not the PHP property names.
     *
     * Mentioning a property here takes it away from laravel-data's rule inferrers, which is the point:
     * these rules are the schema, theirs are a guess from the PHP type.
     *
     * @return array<string, array<int, mixed>>
     */
    public static function rules(): array
    {
        return [
            'test2' => self::withBlankStringValidation(['present', 'string']),
        ];
    }

    /**
     * Laravel skips ordinary rules for blank strings. OpenAPI treats them as present values, so an
     * implicit rule checks only that skipped case, using the same native rules and translator.
     *
     * @param array<int, mixed> $rules
     * @return array<int, mixed>
     */
    private static function withBlankStringValidation(array $rules): array
    {
        $rules[] = new class($rules) implements ValidationRule, ValidatorAwareRule {
            /** Runs for a blank string too, which Laravel skips for an ordinary rule. */
            public bool $implicit = true;

            private ?Validator $validator = null;

            /** @param array<int, mixed> $rules */
            public function __construct(private readonly array $rules)
            {
            }

            public function setValidator(Validator $validator): static
            {
                $this->validator = $validator;

                return $this;
            }

            public function validate(string $attribute, mixed $value, Closure $fail): void
            {
                if (!is_string($value) || trim($value) !== '') {
                    return;
                }
                if ($this->validator === null) {
                    throw new LogicException('The blank-string rule requires a validator.');
                }

                $validator = new class(
                    translator: $this->validator->getTranslator(),
                    data: ['value' => $value],
                    rules: ['value' => $this->rules],
                    attributes: ['value' => $this->validator->getDisplayableAttribute($attribute)],
                ) extends Validator {
                    /**
                     * @param object|string $rule
                     * @param string $attribute
                     * @param mixed $value
                     */
                    protected function presentOrRuleIsImplicit($rule, $attribute, $value): bool
                    {
                        return $this->validatePresent($attribute, $value) || $this->isImplicit($rule);
                    }
                };
                if ($validator->passes()) {
                    return;
                }
                foreach ($validator->errors()->all() as $message) {
                    $fail($message);
                }
            }

            public function __toString(): string
            {
                return 'openapi_blank_string';
            }
        };

        return $rules;
    }
}

===== ApiTest2PatchRequest.php =====
<?php

declare(strict_types=1);

namespace GoldenCorpus;

use Closure;
use Illuminate\Container\Container;
use Illuminate\Contracts\Validation\ValidationRule;
use Illuminate\Contracts\Validation\ValidatorAwareRule;
use Illuminate\Http\Request;
use Illuminate\Validation\Validator;
use LogicException;
use Spatie\LaravelData\Data;

use function array_key_exists;
use function array_keys;
use function array_shift;
use function is_array;
use function is_bool;
use function is_finite;
use function is_float;
use function is_string;
use function json_decode;
use function ltrim;
use function preg_split;
use function str_replace;
use function str_starts_with;
use function trim;

/**
 * NOTE: This DTO is auto-generated from an OpenAPI schema.
 * Do not edit this file manually.
 *
 * Route: PATCH /api/test2
 *
 * laravel-data mode: one class, hydrated and validated by `spatie/laravel-data`.
 *
 *     $dto = ApiTest2PatchRequest::from($request);
 *
 * Validation runs on that call because `validation_strategy` defaults to `OnlyRequests`. Given a plain
 * array instead, use `ApiTest2PatchRequest::validateAndCreate($payload)` — `from($array)` hydrates WITHOUT
 * validating.
 */
final class ApiTest2PatchRequest extends Data
{
    public function __construct(
        public readonly int $idPatchTest2,
        public readonly string $namePatchTest2,
    ) {
    }

    /**
     * Validation rules for the framework's validator, keyed by the OpenAPI property names — which is
     * what the request payload carries, not the PHP property names.
     *
     * Mentioning a property here takes it away from laravel-data's rule inferrers, which is the point:
     * these rules are the schema, theirs are a guess from the PHP type.
     *
     * @return array<string, array<int, mixed>>
     */
    public static function rules(): array
    {
        return [
            'idPatchTest2' => self::withBlankStringValidation(['present', 'integer']),
            'namePatchTest2' => self::withBlankStringValidation(['present', 'string']),
        ];
    }

    /**
     * Laravel skips ordinary rules for blank strings. OpenAPI treats them as present values, so an
     * implicit rule checks only that skipped case, using the same native rules and translator.
     *
     * @param array<int, mixed> $rules
     * @return array<int, mixed>
     */
    private static function withBlankStringValidation(array $rules): array
    {
        $rules[] = new class($rules) implements ValidationRule, ValidatorAwareRule {
            /** Runs for a blank string too, which Laravel skips for an ordinary rule. */
            public bool $implicit = true;

            private ?Validator $validator = null;

            /** @param array<int, mixed> $rules */
            public function __construct(private readonly array $rules)
            {
            }

            public function setValidator(Validator $validator): static
            {
                $this->validator = $validator;

                return $this;
            }

            public function validate(string $attribute, mixed $value, Closure $fail): void
            {
                if (!is_string($value) || trim($value) !== '') {
                    return;
                }
                if ($this->validator === null) {
                    throw new LogicException('The blank-string rule requires a validator.');
                }

                $validator = new class(
                    translator: $this->validator->getTranslator(),
                    data: ['value' => $value],
                    rules: ['value' => $this->rules],
                    attributes: ['value' => $this->validator->getDisplayableAttribute($attribute)],
                ) extends Validator {
                    /**
                     * @param object|string $rule
                     * @param string $attribute
                     * @param mixed $value
                     */
                    protected function presentOrRuleIsImplicit($rule, $attribute, $value): bool
                    {
                        return $this->validatePresent($attribute, $value) || $this->isImplicit($rule);
                    }
                };
                if ($validator->passes()) {
                    return;
                }
                foreach ($validator->errors()->all() as $message) {
                    $fail($message);
                }
            }

            public function __toString(): string
            {
                return 'openapi_blank_string';
            }
        };

        return $rules;
    }

    /**
     * Paths whose schema is `integer` or `number`, with that type. A `*` segment matches every index of
     * an array and every key of a map.
     *
     * @var array<string, string>
     */
    private const array OPENAPI_SCALAR_PATHS = [
        'idPatchTest2' => 'integer',
    ];

    /**
     * The one value Laravel's rules cannot refuse: a JSON boolean where the schema asks for a number.
     *
     * laravel-data calls this itself, and only on the ROOT object: a nested `Data` class never gets its
     * own `withValidator()` run, which is why every check above is flattened to a path from the root.
     */
    public static function withValidator(Validator $validator): void
    {
        $validator->after(static function (Validator $validator): void {
            self::assertNoBooleanNumbers($validator);

            $rawJson = self::rawRequestBody($validator);
            // The body is an object: a non-empty JSON array at the root is refused — it decodes to the PHP
            // array an object keyed 0..n-1 does, so no rule can see it. `[]` is the empty object, as it is
            // nested: `json_encode([])` is how every PHP client sends one, `postJson()` with no data too.
            if ($rawJson !== null && str_starts_with(ltrim($rawJson), '[')) {
                $decoded = json_decode($rawJson, false);
                if (is_array($decoded) && $decoded !== []) {
                    $validator->errors()->add('payload', 'JSON body must be an object, got array.');
                }
            }
        });
    }

    /**
     * The undecoded request body — or null when there is no request, or when what the validator holds is
     * not that body. `validate($array)` on a response and `validateAndCreate()` on one item of a list run
     * this same method while a request is bound, and its body says nothing about their data: a request
     * `{"meta":[1]}` made a response with an object `meta` fail. `from($request)` validates the request's
     * input, which is the body merged with the query, so every key of the body is there unchanged.
     */
    private static function rawRequestBody(Validator $validator): ?string
    {
        $container = Container::getInstance();
        if (!$container->bound('request')) {
            return null;
        }

        $request = $container->make('request');
        if (!$request instanceof Request) {
            return null;
        }

        $content = $request->getContent();
        $body = json_decode($content, true);
        if (!is_array($body)) {
            return null;
        }

        // `getData()` holds every `.` and `*` of a key as a placeholder; the body spells them literally.
        /** @var array<array-key, mixed> $data */
        $data = (fn(): array => $this->replacePlaceholders($this->getData()))->call($validator);
        foreach ($body as $key => $value) {
            if (!array_key_exists($key, $data) || $data[$key] != $value) {
                return null;
            }
        }

        return $content;
    }

    /**
     * Adds an error for every integer or number path that holds a boolean. Laravel's `integer` rule reads
     * `true` through `filter_var()` as 1, so it passed, and laravel-data then cast it to 1 or stored it
     * as `true` in an `array<int>`. Read from the validator's data, not the raw body: a boolean is still
     * a boolean once decoded, so `validateAndCreate($array)` is covered too. A path a rule already
     * failed is left alone, so one mistake is reported once.
     */
    private static function assertNoBooleanNumbers(Validator $validator): void
    {
        /** @var array<array-key, mixed> $data */
        $data = (fn(): array => $this->replacePlaceholders($this->getData()))->call($validator);

        foreach (self::OPENAPI_SCALAR_PATHS as $path => $type) {
            // A nested class reports its boolean paths too; a boolean there is what the schema asks for.
            if ($type === 'boolean') {
                continue;
            }

            // `\.` is a dot INSIDE a property name, as in `rules()`: it splits nothing.
            $segments = str_replace('\\.', '.', preg_split('/(?<!\\\\)\\./', $path) ?: []);
            foreach (self::dataNodesAt($data, $segments, '') as $foundPath => $node) {
                // A number past the float range (`1e400`) decodes to INF, which `numeric` takes and no
                // JSON encoder can write back.
                if ((is_bool($node) || (is_float($node) && !is_finite($node))) && !$validator->errors()->has($foundPath)) {
                    $validator->addFailure($foundPath, $type === 'number' ? 'numeric' : 'integer');
                }
            }
        }
    }

    /**
     * Every value a (possibly wildcarded) path reaches in decoded data, keyed by the concrete path.
     *
     * @param array<int, string> $segments
     * @return array<string, mixed>
     */
    private static function dataNodesAt(mixed $node, array $segments, string $path): array
    {
        if ($segments === []) {
            return [$path => $node];
        }
        if (!is_array($node)) {
            return [];
        }

        $segment = array_shift($segments);
        // `*` stands for both an array index and a map key.
        $keys = $segment === '*' ? array_keys($node) : (array_key_exists($segment, $node) ? [$segment] : []);
        $found = [];
        foreach ($keys as $key) {
            $keyPath = $path === '' ? (string)$key : $path . '.' . $key;
            foreach (self::dataNodesAt($node[$key], $segments, $keyPath) as $deepPath => $deepNode) {
                $found[$deepPath] = $deepNode;
            }
        }

        return $found;
    }
}

                    return;
                }
                if ($this->validator === null) {
                    throw new LogicException('The blank-string rule requires a validator.');
                }

                $validator = new class(
                    translator: $this->validator->getTranslator(),
                    data: ['value' => $value],
                    rules: ['value' => $this->rules],
                    attributes: ['value' => $this->validator->getDisplayableAttribute($attribute)],
                ) extends Validator {
                    /**
                     * @param object|string $rule
                     * @param string $attribute
                     * @param mixed $value
                     */
                    protected function presentOrRuleIsImplicit($rule, $attribute, $value): bool
                    {
                        return $this->validatePresent($attribute, $value) || $this->isImplicit($rule);
                    }
                };
                if ($validator->passes()) {
                    return;
                }
                foreach ($validator->errors()->all() as $message) {
                    $fail($message);
                }
            }

            public function __toString(): string
            {
                return 'openapi_blank_string';
            }
        };

        return $rules;
    }

    /**
     * Paths whose schema is `integer` or `number`, with that type. A `*` segment matches every index of
     * an array and every key of a map.
     *
     * @var array<string, string>
     */
    private const array OPENAPI_SCALAR_PATHS = [
        'idPostTest2' => 'integer',
    ];

    /**
     * The one value Laravel's rules cannot refuse: a JSON boolean where the schema asks for a number.
     *
     * laravel-data calls this itself, and only on the ROOT object: a nested `Data` class never gets its
     * own `withValidator()` run, which is why every check above is flattened to a path from the root.
     */
    public static function withValidator(Validator $validator): void
    {
        $validator->after(static function (Validator $validator): void {
            self::assertNoBooleanNumbers($validator);

            $rawJson = self::rawRequestBody($validator);
            // The body is an object: a non-empty JSON array at the root is refused — it decodes to the PHP
            // array an object keyed 0..n-1 does, so no rule can see it. `[]` is the empty object, as it is
            // nested: `json_encode([])` is how every PHP client sends one, `postJson()` with no data too.
            if ($rawJson !== null && str_starts_with(ltrim($rawJson), '[')) {
                $decoded = json_decode($rawJson, false);
                if (is_array($decoded) && $decoded !== []) {
                    $validator->errors()->add('payload', 'JSON body must be an object, got array.');
                }
            }
        });
    }

    /**
     * The undecoded request body — or null when there is no request, or when what the validator holds is
     * not that body. `validate($array)` on a response and `validateAndCreate()` on one item of a list run
     * this same method while a request is bound, and its body says nothing about their data: a request
     * `{"meta":[1]}` made a response with an object `meta` fail. `from($request)` validates the request's
     * input, which is the body merged with the query, so every key of the body is there unchanged.
     */
    private static function rawRequestBody(Validator $validator): ?string
    {
        $container = Container::getInstance();
        if (!$container->bound('request')) {
            return null;
        }

        $request = $container->make('request');
        if (!$request instanceof Request) {
            return null;
        }

        $content = $request->getContent();
        $body = json_decode($content, true);
        if (!is_array($body)) {
            return null;
        }

        // `getData()` holds every `.` and `*` of a key as a placeholder; the body spells them literally.
        /** @var array<array-key, mixed> $data */
        $data = (fn(): array => $this->replacePlaceholders($this->getData()))->call($validator);
        foreach ($body as $key => $value) {
            if (!array_key_exists($key, $data) || $data[$key] != $value) {
                return null;
            }
        }

        return $content;
    }

    /**
     * Adds an error for every integer or number path that holds a boolean. Laravel's `integer` rule reads
     * `true` through `filter_var()` as 1, so it passed, and laravel-data then cast it to 1 or stored it
     * as `true` in an `array<int>`. Read from the validator's data, not the raw body: a boolean is still
     * a boolean once decoded, so `validateAndCreate($array)` is covered too. A path a rule already
     * failed is left alone, so one mistake is reported once.
     */
    private static function assertNoBooleanNumbers(Validator $validator): void
    {
        /** @var array<array-key, mixed> $data */
        $data = (fn(): array => $this->replacePlaceholders($this->getData()))->call($validator);

        foreach (self::OPENAPI_SCALAR_PATHS as $path => $type) {
            // A nested class reports its boolean paths too; a boolean there is what the schema asks for.
            if ($type === 'boolean') {
                continue;
            }

            // `\.` is a dot INSIDE a property name, as in `rules()`: it splits nothing.
            $segments = str_replace('\\.', '.', preg_split('/(?<!\\\\)\\./', $path) ?: []);
            foreach (self::dataNodesAt($data, $segments, '') as $foundPath => $node) {
                // A number past the float range (`1e400`) decodes to INF, which `numeric` takes and no
                // JSON encoder can write back.
                if ((is_bool($node) || (is_float($node) && !is_finite($node))) && !$validator->errors()->has($foundPath)) {
                    $validator->addFailure($foundPath, $type === 'number' ? 'numeric' : 'integer');
                }
            }
        }
    }

    /**
     * Every value a (possibly wildcarded) path reaches in decoded data, keyed by the concrete path.
     *
     * @param array<int, string> $segments
     * @return array<string, mixed>
     */
    private static function dataNodesAt(mixed $node, array $segments, string $path): array
    {
        if ($segments === []) {
            return [$path => $node];
        }
        if (!is_array($node)) {
            return [];
        }

        $segment = array_shift($segments);
        // `*` stands for both an array index and a map key.
        $keys = $segment === '*' ? array_keys($node) : (array_key_exists($segment, $node) ? [$segment] : []);
        $found = [];
        foreach ($keys as $key) {
            $keyPath = $path === '' ? (string)$key : $path . '.' . $key;
            foreach (self::dataNodesAt($node[$key], $segments, $keyPath) as $deepPath => $deepNode) {
                $found[$deepPath] = $deepNode;
            }
        }

        return $found;
    }
}

            'limit' => self::withBlankStringValidation(['sometimes', 'integer', 'min:1', 'max:100']),
        ];
    }

    /**
     * Laravel skips ordinary rules for blank strings. OpenAPI treats them as present values, so an
     * implicit rule checks only that skipped case, using the same native rules and translator.
     *
     * @param array<int, mixed> $rules
     * @return array<int, mixed>
     */
    private static function withBlankStringValidation(array $rules): array
    {
        $rules[] = new class($rules) implements ValidationRule, ValidatorAwareRule {
            /** Runs for a blank string too, which Laravel skips for an ordinary rule. */
            public bool $implicit = true;

            private ?Validator $validator = null;

            /** @param array<int, mixed> $rules */
            public function __construct(private readonly array $rules)
            {
            }

            public function setValidator(Validator $validator): static
            {
                $this->validator = $validator;

                return $this;
            }

            public function validate(string $attribute, mixed $value, Closure $fail): void
            {
                if (!is_string($value) || trim($value) !== '') {
                    return;
                }
                if ($this->validator === null) {
                    throw new LogicException('The blank-string rule requires a validator.');
                }

                $validator = new class(
                    translator: $this->validator->getTranslator(),
                    data: ['value' => $value],
                    rules: ['value' => $this->rules],
                    attributes: ['value' => $this->validator->getDisplayableAttribute($attribute)],
                ) extends Validator {
                    /**
                     * @param object|string $rule
                     * @param string $attribute
                     * @param mixed $value
                     */

                    {
                        return $this->validatePresent($attribute, $value) || $this->isImplicit($rule);
                    }
                };

                    return;
                }
                foreach ($validator->errors()->all() as $message) {
                    $fail($message);

===== ApiTestFilePostRequest.php =====
<?php

declare(strict_types=1);

namespace GoldenCorpus;

use Closure;
use Illuminate\Container\Container;
use Illuminate\Contracts\Validation\ValidationRule;
use Illuminate\Contracts\Validation\ValidatorAwareRule;
use Illuminate\Http\Request;
use Illuminate\Validation\Validator;
use LogicException;
use Spatie\LaravelData\Data;
use Symfony\Component\HttpFoundation\File\UploadedFile;

use function array_key_exists;
use function is_array;
use function is_string;
use function json_decode;
use function ltrim;
use function str_starts_with;
use function trim;

/**
 * NOTE: This DTO is auto-generated from an OpenAPI schema.
 * Do not edit this file manually.
 *
 * Route: POST /api/test/file
 *
 * laravel-data mode: one class, hydrated and validated by `spatie/laravel-data`.
 *
 *     $dto = ApiTestFilePostRequest::from($request);
 *
 * Validation runs on that call because `validation_strategy` defaults to `OnlyRequests`. Given a plain
 * array instead, use `ApiTestFilePostRequest::validateAndCreate($payload)` — `from($array)` hydrates WITHOUT
 * validating.
 */
final class ApiTestFilePostRequest extends Data
{
    public function __construct(
        public readonly UploadedFile $file,
    ) {
    }

    /**
     * Validation rules for the framework's validator, keyed by the OpenAPI property names — which is
     * what the request payload carries, not the PHP property names.
     *
     * Mentioning a property here takes it away from laravel-data's rule inferrers, which is the point:
     * these rules are the schema, theirs are a guess from the PHP type.
     *
     * @return array<string, array<int, mixed>>
     */
    public static function rules(): array
    {
        return [
            'file' => self::withBlankStringValidation(['present', 'file']),
        ];
    }

    /**
     * Laravel skips ordinary rules for blank strings. OpenAPI treats them as present values, so an
     * implicit rule checks only that skipped case, using the same native rules and translator.
     *
     * @param array<int, mixed> $rules
     * @return array<int, mixed>
     */
    private static function withBlankStringValidation(array $rules): array
    {
        $rules[] = new class($rules) implements ValidationRule, ValidatorAwareRule {
            /** Runs for a blank string too, which Laravel skips for an ordinary rule. */
            public bool $implicit = true;

            private ?Validator $validator = null;

            /** @param array<int, mixed> $rules */
            public function __construct(private readonly array $rules)
            {
            }

            public function setValidator(Validator $validator): static
            {
                $this->validator = $validator;

                return $this;
            }

            public function validate(string $attribute, mixed $value, Closure $fail): void
            {
                if (!is_string($value) || trim($value) !== '') {
                    return;
                }
                if ($this->validator === null) {
                    throw new LogicException('The blank-string rule requires a validator.');
                }

                $validator = new class(
                    translator: $this->validator->getTranslator(),
                    data: ['value' => $value],
                    rules: ['value' => $this->rules],
                    attributes: ['value' => $this->validator->getDisplayableAttribute($attribute)],
                ) extends Validator {
                    /**
                     * @param object|string $rule
                     * @param string $attribute
                     * @param mixed $value
                     */
                    protected function presentOrRuleIsImplicit($rule, $attribute, $value): bool
                    {
                        return $this->validatePresent($attribute, $value) || $this->isImplicit($rule);
                    }
                };
                if ($validator->passes()) {
                    return;
                }
                foreach ($validator->errors()->all() as $message) {
                    $fail($message);
                }
            }

            public function __toString(): string
            {
                return 'openapi_blank_string';
            }
        };

        return $rules;
    }

    /**
     * The one shape Laravel's rules cannot check: a JSON array sent for the object this body is.
     *
     * laravel-data calls this itself, and only on the ROOT object: a nested `Data` class never gets its
     * own `withValidator()` run, which is why every check above is flattened to a path from the root.
     */
    public static function withValidator(Validator $validator): void
    {
        $validator->after(static function (Validator $validator): void {
            $rawJson = self::rawRequestBody($validator);
            // The body is an object: a non-empty JSON array at the root is refused — it decodes to the PHP
            // array an object keyed 0..n-1 does, so no rule can see it. `[]` is the empty object, as it is
            // nested: `json_encode([])` is how every PHP client sends one, `postJson()` with no data too.
            if ($rawJson !== null && str_starts_with(ltrim($rawJson), '[')) {
                $decoded = json_decode($rawJson, false);
                if (is_array($decoded) && $decoded !== []) {
                    $validator->errors()->add('payload', 'JSON body must be an object, got array.');
                }
            }
        });
    }

    /**
     * The undecoded request body — or null when there is no request, or when what the validator holds is
     * not that body. `validate($array)` on a response and `validateAndCreate()` on one item of a list run
     * this same method while a request is bound, and its body says nothing about their data: a request
     * `{"meta":[1]}` made a response with an object `meta` fail. `from($request)` validates the request's
     * input, which is the body merged with the query, so every key of the body is there unchanged.
     */
    private static function rawRequestBody(Validator $validator): ?string
    {
        $container = Container::getInstance();
        if (!$container->bound('request')) {
            return null;
        }

        $request = $container->make('request');
        if (!$request instanceof Request) {
            return null;
        }

        $content = $request->getContent();
        $body = json_decode($content, true);
        if (!is_array($body)) {
            return null;
        }

        // `getData()` holds every `.` and `*` of a key as a placeholder; the body spells them literally.
        /** @var array<array-key, mixed> $data */
        $data = (fn(): array => $this->replacePlaceholders($this->getData()))->call($validator);
        foreach ($body as $key => $value) {
            if (!array_key_exists($key, $data) || $data[$key] != $value) {
                return null;
            }
        }

        return $content;
    }
}

                    protected function presentOrRuleIsImplicit($rule, $attribute, $value): bool
                    {
                        return $this->validatePresent($attribute, $value) || $this->isImplicit($rule);
                    }
                };
                if ($validator->passes()) {
                    return;
                }
                foreach ($validator->errors()->all() as $message) {
                    $fail($message);
                }
            }

            }

namespace GoldenCorpus;

use Closure;

use Illuminate\Contracts\Validation\ValidationRule;

use Spatie\LaravelData\Data;

use function array_key_exists;
use function is_array;
use function is_string;
use function json_decode;
use function ltrim;
use function str_starts_with;
use function trim;

/**
 * NOTE: This DTO is auto-generated from an OpenAPI schema.
 * Do not edit this file manually.
 *
 * Route: POST /api/test/jobs
 *
 * laravel-data mode: one class, hydrated and validated by `spatie/laravel-data`.
 *
 *     $dto = ApiTestJobsPostRequest::from($request);
 *
 * Validation runs on that call because `validation_strategy` defaults to `OnlyRequests`. Given a plain
 * array instead, use `ApiTestJobsPostRequest::validateAndCreate($payload)` — `from($array)` hydrates WITHOUT
 * validating.
 */
final class ApiTestJobsPostRequest extends Data
{
    public function __construct(
        public readonly string $callbackUrl,
    ) {
    }

    /**
     * Validation rules for the framework's validator, keyed by the OpenAPI property names — which is
     * what the request payload carries, not the PHP property names.
     *
     * Mentioning a property here takes it away from laravel-data's rule inferrers, which is the point:
     * these rules are the schema, theirs are a guess from the PHP type.
     *
     * @return array<string, array<int, mixed>>
     */
    public static function rules(): array
    {
        return [
            'callbackUrl' => self::withBlankStringValidation(['present', 'string']),
        ];
    }

    /**
     * Laravel skips ordinary rules for blank strings. OpenAPI treats them as present values, so an
     * implicit rule checks only that skipped case, using the same native rules and translator.
     *
     * @param array<int, mixed> $rules
     * @return array<int, mixed>
     */
    private static function withBlankStringValidation(array $rules): array
    {
        $rules[] = new class($rules) implements ValidationRule, ValidatorAwareRule {
            /** Runs for a blank string too, which Laravel skips for an ordinary rule. */
            public bool $implicit = true;

            private ?Validator $validator = null;

            /** @param array<int, mixed> $rules */
            public function __construct(private readonly array $rules)
            {
            }

            public function setValidator(Validator $validator): static
            {
                $this->validator = $validator;

                return $this;
            }

            public function validate(string $attribute, mixed $value, Closure $fail): void
            {
                if (!is_string($value) || trim($value) !== '') {
                    return;
                }
                if ($this->validator === null) {
                    throw new LogicException('The blank-string rule requires a validator.');
                }

                $validator = new class(
                    translator: $this->validator->getTranslator(),
                    data: ['value' => $value],
                    rules: ['value' => $this->rules],
                    attributes: ['value' => $this->validator->getDisplayableAttribute($attribute)],
                ) extends Validator {
                    /**
                     * @param object|string $rule
                     * @param string $attribute
                     * @param mixed $value
                     */
                    protected function presentOrRuleIsImplicit($rule, $attribute, $value): bool
                    {
                        return $this->validatePresent($attribute, $value) || $this->isImplicit($rule);
                    }
                };
                if ($validator->passes()) {
                    return;
                }
                foreach ($validator->errors()->all() as $message) {
                    $fail($message);
                }
            }

            public function __toString(): string
            {
                return 'openapi_blank_string';
            }
        };

        return $rules;
    }

    /**
     * The one shape Laravel's rules cannot check: a JSON array sent for the object this body is.
     *
     * laravel-data calls this itself, and only on the ROOT object: a nested `Data` class never gets its
     * own `withValidator()` run, which is why every check above is flattened to a path from the root.
     */
    public static function withValidator(Validator $validator): void
    {
        $validator->after(static function (Validator $validator): void {
            $rawJson = self::rawRequestBody($validator);
            // The body is an object: a non-empty JSON array at the root is refused — it decodes to the PHP
            // array an object keyed 0..n-1 does, so no rule can see it. `[]` is the empty object, as it is
            // nested: `json_encode([])` is how every PHP client sends one, `postJson()` with no data too.
            if ($rawJson !== null && str_starts_with(ltrim($rawJson), '[')) {
                $decoded = json_decode($rawJson, false);
                if (is_array($decoded) && $decoded !== []) {
                    $validator->errors()->add('payload', 'JSON body must be an object, got array.');
                }
            }
        });
    }

    /**
     * The undecoded request body — or null when there is no request, or when what the validator holds is
     * not that body. `validate($array)` on a response and `validateAndCreate()` on one item of a list run
     * this same method while a request is bound, and its body says nothing about their data: a request
     * `{"meta":[1]}` made a response with an object `meta` fail. `from($request)` validates the request's
     * input, which is the body merged with the query, so every key of the body is there unchanged.
     */
    private static function rawRequestBody(Validator $validator): ?string
    {
        $container = Container::getInstance();
        if (!$container->bound('request')) {
            return null;
        }

        $request = $container->make('request');
        if (!$request instanceof Request) {
            return null;
        }

        $content = $request->getContent();
        $body = json_decode($content, true);
        if (!is_array($body)) {
            return null;
        }

        // `getData()` holds every `.` and `*` of a key as a placeholder; the body spells them literally.
        /** @var array<array-key, mixed> $data */
        $data = (fn(): array => $this->replacePlaceholders($this->getData()))->call($validator);
        foreach ($body as $key => $value) {
            if (!array_key_exists($key, $data) || $data[$key] != $value) {
                return null;
            }
        }

        return $content;
    }
}

use Spatie\LaravelData\Optional;

use function array_key_exists;
use function array_map;
use function explode;
use function filter_var;
use function is_array;
use function is_numeric;
use function is_string;
use function trim;

/**
 * NOTE: This DTO is auto-generated from an OpenAPI schema.
 * Do not edit this file manually.
 *
 * @deprecated
 *
 * Route: POST /api/test/legacy
 *
 * laravel-data mode: one class, hydrated and validated by `spatie/laravel-data`.
 *
 *     $dto = ApiTestLegacyPostQueryParams::from($request);
 *
 * Validation runs on that call because `validation_strategy` defaults to `OnlyRequests`. Given a plain
 * array instead, use `ApiTestLegacyPostQueryParams::validateAndCreate($payload)` — `from($array)` hydrates WITHOUT
 * validating.
 *
 * An optional property is typed `…|Optional`, so an unprovided one IS a `Spatie\LaravelData\Optional`
 * and is omitted from `toArray()`. That keeps "absent" and "sent as null" apart without any bookkeeping
 * of ours — PATCH semantics as a language-level fact:
 *
 *     if (! $dto->legacyFlag instanceof Optional) { … }
 */
final class ApiTestLegacyPostQueryParams extends Data
{
    public function __construct(
        public readonly bool|Optional $legacyFlag = new Optional(),
    ) {
    }

    /**
     * The query and path parameters as the document serializes them, read before laravel-data validates
     * or hydrates anything: `?ids=1,2` for an `explode: false` list, `?active=true` for a boolean,
     * `?levels[]=2` for a list of ints. Its own pipeline hands everything over as strings.
     *
     * @param array<array-key, mixed> $properties
     * @return array<array-key, mixed>
     */
    public static function prepareForPipeline(array $properties): array
    {
        if (array_key_exists('legacyFlag', $properties)) {
            $properties['legacyFlag'] = self::openApiParameterValue($properties['legacyFlag'], 'bool');
        }

        return $properties;
    }

    /**
     * A list parameter as its `style` sends it: joined by the delimiter — `?ids=1,2` — or, for `form`
     * exploded, one value per key, where a lone `?ids=1` is a list of one. Each item is then read as
     * {@see openApiParameterValue()} reads a value.
     */
    private static function openApiParameterList(mixed $value, ?string $delimiter, string $kind): mixed
    {
        if (is_string($value)) {
            $value = match (true) {
                $delimiter === null => [$value],
                $value === '' => [],
                default => explode($delimiter, $value),
            };
        }
        if (!is_array($value)) {
            return $value;
        }

        return array_map(static fn(mixed $item): mixed => self::openApiParameterValue($item, $kind), $value);
    }

    /**
     * A parameter value is a string; this is the value it stands for — `"7"` an int, `"true"` a bool. A
     * string that is not one is left as it came, for the rules to refuse.
     */
    private static function openApiParameterValue(mixed $value, string $kind): mixed
    {
        if (!is_string($value)) {
            return $value;
        }

        return match ($kind) {
            'int' => filter_var($value, FILTER_VALIDATE_INT, FILTER_NULL_ON_FAILURE) ?? $value,
            'float' => is_numeric($value) ? $value + 0 : $value,
            'bool' => match ($value) {
                'true', '1' => true,
                'false', '0' => false,
                default => $value,
            },
            default => $value,
        };
    }

    /**
     * Validation rules for the framework's validator, keyed by the OpenAPI property names — which is
     * what the request payload carries, not the PHP property names.
     *
     * Mentioning a property here takes it away from laravel-data's rule inferrers, which is the point:
     * these rules are the schema, theirs are a guess from the PHP type.
     *
     * @return array<string, array<int, mixed>>
     */
    public static function rules(): array
    {
        return [
            'legacyFlag' => self::withBlankStringValidation(['sometimes', 'boolean']),
        ];
    }

    /**
     * Laravel skips ordinary rules for blank strings. OpenAPI treats them as present values, so an
     * implicit rule checks only that skipped case, using the same native rules and translator.
     *
     * @param array<int, mixed> $rules
     * @return array<int, mixed>
     */
    private static function withBlankStringValidation(array $rules): array
    {
        $rules[] = new class($rules) implements ValidationRule, ValidatorAwareRule {
            /** Runs for a blank string too, which Laravel skips for an ordinary rule. */
            public bool $implicit = true;

            private ?Validator $validator = null;

            /** @param array<int, mixed> $rules */
            public function __construct(private readonly array $rules)
            {
            }

            public function setValidator(Validator $validator): static
            {
                $this->validator = $validator;

                return $this;
            }

            public function validate(string $attribute, mixed $value, Closure $fail): void
            {

                    return;

                $validator = new class(

    public static function rules(): array

     * Laravel skips ordinary rules for blank strings. OpenAPI treats them as present values, so an
     * implicit rule checks only that skipped case, using the same native rules and translator.
     *
     * @param array<int, mixed> $rules
     * @return array<int, mixed>
     */
    private static function withBlankStringValidation(array $rules): array
    {

            /** Runs for a blank string too, which Laravel skips for an ordinary rule. */
            public bool $implicit = true;

            private ?Validator $validator = null;

            /** @param array<int, mixed> $rules */
            public function __construct(private readonly array $rules)
            {
            }

            public function setValidator(Validator $validator): static
            {
                $this->validator = $validator;

                return $this;
            }

            public function validate(string $attribute, mixed $value, Closure $fail): void
            {
                if (!is_string($value) || trim($value) !== '') {
                    return;
                }
                if ($this->validator === null) {

                }

                $validator = new class(
                    translator: $this->validator->getTranslator(),
                    data: ['value' => $value],
                    rules: ['value' => $this->rules],
                    attributes: ['value' => $this->validator->getDisplayableAttribute($attribute)],
                ) extends Validator {

                     * @param object|string $rule
                     * @param string $attribute
                     * @param mixed $value
                     */

                    }
                };

                }
            }

            public function __toString(): string
            {
                return 'openapi_blank_string';
            }
        };

        return $rules;
    }

    /**
     * The one shape Laravel's rules cannot check: a JSON array sent for the object this body is.
     *
     * laravel-data calls this itself, and only on the ROOT object: a nested `Data` class never gets its
     * own `withValidator()` run, which is why every check above is flattened to a path from the root.
     */
    public static function withValidator(Validator $validator): void
    {
        $validator->after(static function (Validator $validator): void {
            $rawJson = self::rawRequestBody($validator);
            // The body is an object: a non-empty JSON array at the root is refused — it decodes to the PHP
            // array an object keyed 0..n-1 does, so no rule can see it. `[]` is the empty object, as it is
            // nested: `json_encode([])` is how every PHP client sends one, `postJson()` with no data too.
            if ($rawJson !== null && str_starts_with(ltrim($rawJson), '[')) {
                $decoded = json_decode($rawJson, false);
                if (is_array($decoded) && $decoded !== []) {
                    $validator->errors()->add('payload', 'JSON body must be an object, got array.');
                }
            }
        });
    }

    /**
     * The undecoded request body — or null when there is no request, or when what the validator holds is
     * not that body. `validate($array)` on a response and `validateAndCreate()` on one item of a list run
     * this same method while a request is bound, and its body says nothing about their data: a request
     * `{"meta":[1]}` made a response with an object `meta` fail. `from($request)` validates the request's
     * input, which is the body merged with the query, so every key of the body is there unchanged.
     */
    private static function rawRequestBody(Validator $validator): ?string
    {
        $container = Container::getInstance();
        if (!$container->bound('request')) {
            return null;
        }

        $request = $container->make('request');
        if (!$request instanceof Request) {
            return null;
        }

        $content = $request->getContent();
        $body = json_decode($content, true);
        if (!is_array($body)) {
            return null;
        }

        // `getData()` holds every `.` and `*` of a key as a placeholder; the body spells them literally.
        /** @var array<array-key, mixed> $data */
        $data = (fn(): array => $this->replacePlaceholders($this->getData()))->call($validator);
        foreach ($body as $key => $value) {
            if (!array_key_exists($key, $data) || $data[$key] != $value) {
                return null;
            }
        }

        return $content;
    }
}

                    protected function presentOrRuleIsImplicit($rule, $attribute, $value): bool
                    {
                        return $this->validatePresent($attribute, $value) || $this->isImplicit($rule);
                    }
                };
                if ($validator->passes()) {
                    return;
                }
                foreach ($validator->errors()->all() as $message) {
                    $fail($message);
                }
            }

            public function __toString(): string
            {
                return 'openapi_blank_string';
            }
        };

        return $rules;
    }

 * An optional property is typed `…|Optional`, so an unprovided one IS a `Spatie\LaravelData\Optional`

 * of ours — PATCH semantics as a language-level fact:
 *
 *     if (! $dto->exploded instanceof Optional) { … }
 */
final class ApiTestSerializationGetQueryParams extends Data
{
    /**
     * @param array<int>|Optional $exploded
     * @param array<string>|Optional $joined
     * @param array<int>|Optional $spaced
     * @param array<string>|Optional $piped
     * @param array<string, string>|Optional $nested
     * @param array<string>|Optional $xCodes
     */
    public function __construct(
        public readonly array|Optional $exploded = new Optional(),
        public readonly array|Optional $joined = new Optional(),
        public readonly array|Optional $spaced = new Optional(),
        public readonly array|Optional $piped = new Optional(),
        public readonly array|Optional $nested = new Optional(),
        public readonly bool|Optional $flag = new Optional(),
        public readonly string|Optional $strict = new Optional(),
        public readonly string|Optional $raw = new Optional(),
        #[MapName('X-Codes')]
        public readonly array|Optional $xCodes = new Optional(),
        public readonly string|Optional $session = new Optional(),
        #[WithoutValidation]
        public readonly ApiTestSerializationGetQueryParamsFilter|Optional $filter = new Optional(),
    ) {
    }

    /**
     * The query and path parameters as the document serializes them, read before laravel-data validates
     * or hydrates anything: `?ids=1,2` for an `explode: false` list, `?active=true` for a boolean,
     * `?levels[]=2` for a list of ints. Its own pipeline hands everything over as strings.
     *
     * @param array<array-key, mixed> $properties
     * @return array<array-key, mixed>
     */
    public static function prepareForPipeline(array $properties): array
    {
        if (array_key_exists('exploded', $properties)) {
            $properties['exploded'] = self::openApiParameterList($properties['exploded'], null, 'int');
        }
        if (array_key_exists('joined', $properties)) {
            $properties['joined'] = self::openApiParameterList($properties['joined'], ',', 'string');
        }
        if (array_key_exists('spaced', $properties)) {
            $properties['spaced'] = self::openApiParameterList($properties['spaced'], ' ', 'int');
        }
        if (array_key_exists('piped', $properties)) {
            $properties['piped'] = self::openApiParameterList($properties['piped'], '|', 'string');
        }
        if (array_key_exists('flag', $properties)) {
            $properties['flag'] = self::openApiParameterValue($properties['flag'], 'bool');
        }
        if (array_key_exists('X-Codes', $properties)) {
            $properties['X-Codes'] = self::openApiParameterList($properties['X-Codes'], ',', 'string');
        }

        return $properties;
    }

    /**
     * A list parameter as its `style` sends it: joined by the delimiter — `?ids=1,2` — or, for `form`
     * exploded, one value per key, where a lone `?ids=1` is a list of one. Each item is then read as
     * {@see openApiParameterValue()} reads a value.
     */
    private static function openApiParameterList(mixed $value, ?string $delimiter, string $kind): mixed
    {
        if (is_string($value)) {
            $value = match (true) {
                $delimiter === null => [$value],
                $value === '' => [],
                default => explode($delimiter, $value),
            };
        }
        if (!is_array($value)) {
            return $value;
        }

        return array_map(static fn(mixed $item): mixed => self::openApiParameterValue($item, $kind), $value);
    }

    /**
     * A parameter value is a string; this is the value it stands for — `"7"` an int, `"true"` a bool. A
     * string that is not one is left as it came, for the rules to refuse.
     */
    private static function openApiParameterValue(mixed $value, string $kind): mixed
    {
        if (!is_string($value)) {
            return $value;
        }

        return match ($kind) {
            'int' => filter_var($value, FILTER_VALIDATE_INT, FILTER_NULL_ON_FAILURE) ?? $value,
            'float' => is_numeric($value) ? $value + 0 : $value,
            'bool' => match ($value) {
                'true', '1' => true,
                'false', '0' => false,
                default => $value,
            },
            default => $value,
        };
    }

    /**
     * Validation rules for the framework's validator, keyed by the OpenAPI property names — which is
     * what the request payload carries, not the PHP property names.
     *
     * Mentioning a property here takes it away from laravel-data's rule inferrers, which is the point:
     * these rules are the schema, theirs are a guess from the PHP type.
     *
     * @return array<string, array<int, mixed>>
     */
    public static function rules(): array
    {
        return [
            'exploded' => self::withBlankStringValidation(['sometimes', 'list']),
            'exploded.*' => self::withBlankStringValidation(['integer']),
            'joined' => self::withBlankStringValidation(['sometimes', 'list']),
            'joined.*' => self::withBlankStringValidation(['string']),
            'spaced' => self::withBlankStringValidation(['sometimes', 'list']),
            'spaced.*' => self::withBlankStringValidation(['integer']),
            'piped' => self::withBlankStringValidation(['sometimes', 'list']),
            'piped.*' => self::withBlankStringValidation(['string']),
            'nested' => self::withBlankStringValidation(['sometimes', 'array']),
            'nested.*' => self::withBlankStringValidation(['string']),
            'flag' => self::withBlankStringValidation(['sometimes', 'boolean']),
            'strict' => self::withBlankStringValidation(['sometimes', 'string']),
            'raw' => self::withBlankStringValidation(['sometimes', 'string']),
            'X-Codes' => self::withBlankStringValidation(['sometimes', 'list']),
            'X-Codes.*' => self::withBlankStringValidation(['string']),
            'session' => self::withBlankStringValidation(['sometimes', 'string']),
            'filter' => self::withBlankStringValidation(['sometimes', 'array']),
            ...self::openApiNestedParts()['rules'],
        ];
    }

    /**
     * Laravel skips ordinary rules for blank strings. OpenAPI treats them as present values, so an
     * implicit rule checks only that skipped case, using the same native rules and translator.
     *
     * @param array<int, mixed> $rules
     * @return array<int, mixed>
     */
    private static function withBlankStringValidation(array $rules): array
    {
        $rules[] = new class($rules) implements ValidationRule, ValidatorAwareRule {
            /** Runs for a blank string too, which Laravel skips for an ordinary rule. */
            public bool $implicit = true;

            private ?Validator $validator = null;

            /** @param array<int, mixed> $rules */
            public function __construct(private readonly array $rules)
            {
            }

            public function setValidator(Validator $validator): static
            {
                $this->validator = $validator;

                return $this;
            }

            public function validate(string $attribute, mixed $value, Closure $fail): void
            {
                if (!is_string($value) || trim($value) !== '') {
                    return;
                }
                if ($this->validator === null) {
                    throw new LogicException('The blank-string rule requires a validator.');
                }

                $validator = new class(
                    translator: $this->validator->getTranslator(),
                    data: ['value' => $value],
                    rules: ['value' => $this->rules],
                    attributes: ['value' => $this->validator->getDisplayableAttribute($attribute)],
                ) extends Validator {
                    /**
                     * @param object|string $rule
                     * @param string $attribute
                     * @param mixed $value
                     */
                    protected function presentOrRuleIsImplicit($rule, $attribute, $value): bool
                    {
                        return $this->validatePresent($attribute, $value) || $this->isImplicit($rule);
                    }
                };
                if ($validator->passes()) {
                    return;
                }
                foreach ($validator->errors()->all() as $message) {
                    $fail($message);
                }
            }

            public function __toString(): string
            {
                return 'openapi_blank_string';
            }
        };

        return $rules;
    }

    /**
     * What the nested DTOs below this class's properties add to its validation — their dotted rules and
     * the paths whose wire shape is checked — asked of each nested class rather than written out here.
     *
     * @return array{rules: array<string, array<int, mixed>>, objectPaths: array<int, string>, scalarPaths: array<string, string>}
     */
    private static function openApiNestedParts(): array
    {
        $parts = [
            ApiTestSerializationGetQueryParamsFilter::openApiNestedValidation('filter', [self::class]),
        ];

        return [
            'rules' => array_merge(...array_column($parts, 'rules')),
            'objectPaths' => array_merge(...array_column($parts, 'objectPaths')),
            'scalarPaths' => array_merge(...array_column($parts, 'scalarPaths')),
        ];
    }

    /**
     * Paths whose schema is `type: object`. A `*` segment matches every index of an array.
     *
     * @var array<int, string>
     */
    private const array OPENAPI_OBJECT_PATHS = [
        'nested',
        'filter',
    ];

    /**
     * The one shape Laravel's rules cannot check: `type: object` given a JSON array.
     *
     * laravel-data calls this itself, and only on the ROOT object: a nested `Data` class never gets its
     * own `withValidator()` run, which is why every check above is flattened to a path from the root.
     *
     * One check needs the UNDECODED body, because `type: object` versus `type: array` is a property of
     * the wire shape that PHP loses — a JSON object keyed 0..n-1 and a JSON array decode to the same
     * list. Only the request still has those bytes, so on a `validateAndCreate($array)` call, where
     * there is no request, that single check is skipped and everything else still runs.
     */
    public static function withValidator(Validator $validator): void
    {
        $validator->after(static function (Validator $validator): void {
            $rawJson = self::rawRequestBody($validator);
            if ($rawJson !== null && $rawJson !== '') {
                self::assertRawObjectShapes($validator, $rawJson);
            }
        });
    }

    /**
     * The undecoded request body — or null when there is no request, or when what the validator holds is
     * not that body. `validate($array)` on a response and `validateAndCreate()` on one item of a list run
     * this same method while a request is bound, and its body says nothing about their data: a request
     * `{"meta":[1]}` made a response with an object `meta` fail. `from($request)` validates the request's
     * input, which is the body merged with the query, so every key of the body is there unchanged.
     */
    private static function rawRequestBody(Validator $validator): ?string
    {
        $container = Container::getInstance();
        if (!$container->bound('request')) {
            return null;
        }

        $request = $container->make('request');
        if (!$request instanceof Request) {
            return null;
        }

        $content = $request->getContent();
        $body = json_decode($content, true);
        if (!is_array($body)) {
            return null;
        }

        // `getData()` holds every `.` and `*` of a key as a placeholder; the body spells them literally.
        /** @var array<array-key, mixed> $data */
        $data = (fn(): array => $this->replacePlaceholders($this->getData()))->call($validator);
        foreach ($body as $key => $value) {
            if (!array_key_exists($key, $data) || $data[$key] != $value) {
                return null;
            }
        }

        return $content;
    }

    /**
     * Adds an error for every `type: object` path the raw body sent as a JSON array.
     */
    private static function assertRawObjectShapes(Validator $validator, string $rawJson): void
    {
        // Decoded WITHOUT the assoc flag on purpose: that is what keeps a JSON object apart from a
        // JSON array. Laravel's own `$validator->getData()` cannot answer this question.
        $decoded = json_decode($rawJson, false);
        if (!$decoded instanceof stdClass) {
            return;
        }

        foreach (self::OPENAPI_OBJECT_PATHS as $path) {
            foreach (self::rawNodesAt($decoded, explode('.', $path), '') as $foundPath => $node) {
                // `[]` is how PHP encodes an empty array, so it is the empty object a PHP client sends.
                if (is_array($node) && $node !== []) {
                    $validator->errors()->add($foundPath, sprintf('%s expects object, got array.', $foundPath));
                }
            }
        }
    }

    /**
     * Every node a (possibly wildcarded) path reaches, keyed by the concrete path that got there.
     *
     * @param array<int, string> $segments
     * @return array<string, mixed>
     */
    private static function rawNodesAt(mixed $node, array $segments, string $path): array
    {
        if ($segments === []) {
            return [$path => $node];
        }

        $segment = array_shift($segments);
        if ($segment === '*') {
            // `*` stands for both an array index and a map key, so a JSON object node is walked too.
            if ($node instanceof stdClass) {
                $node = get_object_vars($node);
            }
            if (!is_array($node)) {
                return [];
            }

            $found = [];
            foreach ($node as $index => $item) {
                $itemPath = $path === '' ? (string)$index : $path . '.' . $index;
                foreach (self::rawNodesAt($item, $segments, $itemPath) as $deepPath => $deepNode) {
                    $found[$deepPath] = $deepNode;
                }
            }

            return $found;
        }

        if (!$node instanceof stdClass || !property_exists($node, $segment)) {
            return [];
        }

        return self::rawNodesAt($node->{$segment}, $segments, $path === '' ? $segment : $path . '.' . $segment);
    }
}

     * Laravel skips ordinary rules for blank strings. OpenAPI treats them as present values, so an
     * implicit rule checks only that skipped case, using the same native rules and translator.
     *
     * @param array<int, mixed> $rules
     * @return array<int, mixed>
     */

    {
        $rules[] = new class($rules) implements ValidationRule, ValidatorAwareRule {
            /** Runs for a blank string too, which Laravel skips for an ordinary rule. */
            public bool $implicit = true;

            private ?Validator $validator = null;

            /** @param array<int, mixed> $rules */
            public function __construct(private readonly array $rules)
            {
            }

            public function setValidator(Validator $validator): static
            {
                $this->validator = $validator;

                return $this;
            }

            public function validate(string $attribute, mixed $value, Closure $fail): void
            {
                if (!is_string($value) || trim($value) !== '') {
                    return;
                }
                if ($this->validator === null) {
                    throw new LogicException('The blank-string rule requires a validator.');
                }

                $validator = new class(
                    translator: $this->validator->getTranslator(),
                    data: ['value' => $value],
                    rules: ['value' => $this->rules],
                    attributes: ['value' => $this->validator->getDisplayableAttribute($attribute)],
                ) extends Validator {
                    /**
                     * @param object|string $rule
                     * @param string $attribute
                     * @param mixed $value
                     */
                    protected function presentOrRuleIsImplicit($rule, $attribute, $value): bool
                    {
                        return $this->validatePresent($attribute, $value) || $this->isImplicit($rule);
                    }
                };
                if ($validator->passes()) {
                    return;
                }
                foreach ($validator->errors()->all() as $message) {
                    $fail($message);
                }
            }

            public function __toString(): string
            {
                return 'openapi_blank_string';
            }
        };

        return $rules;
    }

    {

     * string that is not one is left as it came, for the rules to refuse.

    private static function openApiParameterValue(mixed $value, string $kind): mixed
    {

            'int' => filter_var($value, FILTER_VALIDATE_INT, FILTER_NULL_ON_FAILURE) ?? $value,

            'bool' => match ($value) {
                'true', '1' => true,
                'false', '0' => false,
                default => $value,
            },
            default => $value,

            'neverMatches' => self::withBlankStringValidation(['sometimes']),
            'closedFirstSlot' => self::withBlankStringValidation(['sometimes', 'list']),
            'poisonedKey' => self::withBlankStringValidation(['sometimes', 'array']),
            'conditional' => self::withBlankStringValidation(['sometimes', 'array']),
            'keyed' => self::withBlankStringValidation(['sometimes', 'array']),
