                    {
                        return $this->validatePresent($attribute, $value) || $this->isImplicit($rule);
                    }
                };
                if ($validator->passes()) {
                    return;
                }
                foreach ($validator->errors()->all() as $message) {
                    $fail($message);
                }
            }

            public function __toString(): string
            {
                return 'openapi_blank_string';
            }
        };

        return $rules;
    }

    /**
     * The half of the schema Laravel's rule vocabulary cannot express — composition, conditionals,
     * `contains`, `unevaluated*`, `content*`, `propertyNames`, `discriminator` — checked against the
     * raw payload the validator already holds.
     *
     * laravel-data calls this itself, and only on the ROOT object: a nested `Data` class never gets its
     * own `withValidator()` run, which is why every check above is flattened to a path from the root.
     */
    public static function withValidator(Validator $validator): void
    {
        $validator->after(static function (Validator $validator): void {
            // `getData()` holds every `.` and `*` of a payload KEY as a placeholder; the schema and the
            // client name them literally, and an error under a dotted map key was reported at a random
            // `__dot__…` path. Laravel mode undoes them the same way.
            /** @var array<string, mixed> $payload */
            $payload = (fn(): array => $this->replacePlaceholders($this->getData()))->call($validator);

            foreach (self::OPENAPI_VALIDATION_CONSTRAINTS['fields'] as $property => $schema) {
                if (!array_key_exists($property, $payload)) {
                    continue;
                }

                foreach (self::validateOpenApiNode($payload[$property], $schema, $property, 0) as $error) {
                    self::addOpenApiFieldError($validator, $property, $payload[$property], $error);
                }
            }

            // What the document states about the OBJECT rather than about one property:
            // `minProperties`, `dependentRequired`, `not`, a top-level conditional. It is asked of the
            // whole payload, so it is keyed `payload` — or by the property it names. `properties` and
            // `required` are not in this schema — the rule map owns them — so
            // nothing here is reported twice.
            foreach (self::validateOpenApiNode($payload, self::OPENAPI_VALIDATION_CONSTRAINTS['object'], 'payload', 0) as $error) {
                $validator->errors()->add(self::openApiObjectErrorKey($error), str_ends_with($error, '.') ? $error : $error . '.');
            }
        });
    }

    /**
     * The error-bag key for a message about the object itself. An undeclared key, or a key a dependency
     * requires, is the field the client got wrong, so it is named; anything else (`minProperties`, `not`,
     * a conditional) is about the whole payload. Before 2.15.55 every such error had the key `""` and
     * a message opening on a space.
     */
    private static function openApiObjectErrorKey(string $error): string
    {
        if (preg_match('/^payload has (?:additional|unevaluated) property "(.*)" which is not allowed/s', $error, $match) === 1) {
            return $match[1];
        }

        // `field "region" is required when code is present` — a dependency names the property it is about.
        return preg_match('/^field "([^"]+)"/', $error, $match) === 1 ? $match[1] : 'payload';
    }

    /**
     * Reports an interpreter message under the field it is about, as Laravel's own rules do: a key
     * missing deep inside a union lands on `shape.radius`, a list item on `levels.0` — the message
     * opens with that path. Before 2.15.91 every one of them was keyed by the top-level property.
     *
     * A claim a Laravel rule also makes — a missing key, a wrong type, a value outside an enum — is
     * reported AS that rule, so it reads like the rest of the error bag and the application's
     * translations apply. Anything else keeps the interpreter's sentence.
     *
     * A path the payload does not lead along — one into a JSON string's decoded `contentSchema` — is not
     * a field of the request, so that message stays on the property with its own sentence.
     */
    private static function addOpenApiFieldError(Validator $validator, string $property, mixed $value, string $error): void
    {
        // `shape.radius is required`, `levels[0] must be one of: 1, 2`: the deeper segments, then the claim.
        $rest = str_starts_with($error, $property) ? substr($error, strlen($property)) : '';
        if (preg_match('/^((?:\.[^ .\[]+|\[\d+\])*) (.+)$/s', $rest, $match) !== 1) {
            $validator->errors()->add($property, str_ends_with($error, '.') ? $error : $error . '.');

            return;
        }

        preg_match_all('/\.([^ .\[]+)|\[(\d+)\]/', $match[1], $found, PREG_SET_ORDER);
        $segments = array_map(static fn(array $segment): string => ($segment[2] ?? '') !== '' ? $segment[2] : $segment[1], $found);
        $node = $value;
        foreach (array_slice($segments, 0, -1) as $segment) {
            if (!is_array($node) || !array_key_exists($segment, $node)) {
                $node = null;
                break;
            }
            $node = $node[$segment];
        }
        if ($segments !== [] && !is_array($node)) {
            $validator->errors()->add($property, str_ends_with($error, '.') ? $error : $error . '.');

            return;
        }

        $path = implode('.', [$property, ...$segments]);
        $claim = $match[2];
        // A field a rule has already failed — `kind` of a discriminated value carries `Rule::enum()`,
        // a union that holds a scalar branch carries `array` — is one mistake, reported once.
        if ($validator->errors()->has($path) && ($segments !== [] || str_starts_with($claim, 'does not match any '))) {
            return;
        }
        // The same for a union whose member's rules already failed below it: `null` for a discriminated
        // union is `pet.kind` required, and "pet does not match any oneOf branch" beside it said it twice.
        if (str_starts_with($claim, 'does not match any ') && $validator->errors()->has($path . '.*')) {
            return;
        }

        $rule = match ($claim) {
            'is required' => 'present',
            'must be of type integer' => 'integer',
            'must be of type number' => 'numeric',
            'must be of type string' => 'string',
            'must be of type boolean' => 'boolean',
            'must be of type array' => 'list',
            'must be of type object' => 'array',
            default => str_starts_with($claim, 'must be one of: ') ? 'enum' : null,
        };

        if ($rule !== null) {
            $validator->addFailure($path, $rule);

            return;
        }

        $validator->errors()->add($path, str_ends_with($error, '.') ? $error : $error . '.');
    }

    /**
     * @param array<string, mixed> $schema
     * @return array<int, string>
     */
    private static function validateOpenApiNode(mixed $value, array $schema, string $path, int $depth): array
    {
        if ($depth > 0) {
            return self::validateOpenApiNodeUnchecked($value, $schema, $path, $depth);
        }
        try {
            return self::validateOpenApiNodeUnchecked($value, $schema, $path, $depth);
        } catch (OverflowException $error) {
            return [$error->getMessage()];
        }
    }

    /**
     * @param array<string, mixed> $schema
     * @return array<int, string>
     */
    private static function validateOpenApiNodeUnchecked(mixed $value, array $schema, string $path, int $depth): array
    {
        // An incomplete check is not a branch mismatch: not/if/anyOf must not invert or swallow it.
        if ($depth >= self::OPENAPI_MAX_VALIDATION_DEPTH) {
            throw new OverflowException($path . ' exceeds maximum validation depth.');
        }

        $errors = [];
        $normalizedValue = $value;
        $structuredValue = self::normalizeOpenApiStructuralValue($value);
        if (
            is_array($structuredValue)
            && $structuredValue !== []
            && array_is_list($structuredValue)
            && (!in_array('object', (array)($schema['type'] ?? []), true) || in_array('array', (array)($schema['type'] ?? []), true))
        ) {
            $structuredValue = null;
        }

        if (array_key_exists('type', $schema)) {
            $typeConstraint = $schema['type'];
            if (is_string($typeConstraint)) {
                if (!self::matchesOpenApiCallbackType($normalizedValue, $typeConstraint)) {
                    $errors[] = sprintf('%s must be of type %s', $path, $typeConstraint);
                }
            }
        }

        if (is_string($normalizedValue) && self::schemaHasAnyOpenApiKey($schema, ['minLength'])) {
            $length = mb_strlen($normalizedValue);
            $minLength = self::toIntConstraint($schema['minLength'] ?? null);
            if ($minLength !== null && $length < $minLength) {
                $errors[] = sprintf('%s length must be at least %d characters', $path, $minLength);
            }
        }

        if (is_array($value) || is_object($value)) {
            $structural = is_array($value) ? $value : self::normalizeOpenApiStructuralValue($value);
            // Presence belongs to the payload view. Null remains a real list item/object value.
            $valueArray = is_array($structural) ? $structural : [];
            $count = count($valueArray);
            // The item keywords speak about a LIST. A JSON object reaches this point as an array with
            // its member names for keys, and `minItems` beside no `type` counted those members as
            // items — refusing an object the keyword says nothing about.
            $isList = is_array($value) && array_is_list($value);
            $minItems = self::toIntConstraint($schema['minItems'] ?? null);
            if ($minItems !== null && $isList && $count < $minItems) {
                $errors[] = sprintf('%s must contain at least %d items', $path, $minItems);
            }

            $maxItems = self::toIntConstraint($schema['maxItems'] ?? null);
            if ($maxItems !== null && $isList && $count > $maxItems) {
                $errors[] = sprintf('%s must contain at most %d items', $path, $maxItems);
            }

            // A non-empty list has no properties to count, unless `type` pins `object` — see
            // `$structuredValue`.
            $countsProperties = !$isList
                || $valueArray === []
                || (in_array('object', (array)($schema['type'] ?? []), true) && !in_array('array', (array)($schema['type'] ?? []), true));
            $minProperties = self::toIntConstraint($schema['minProperties'] ?? null);
            if ($minProperties !== null && $countsProperties && $count < $minProperties) {
                $errors[] = sprintf('%s must have at least %d %s', $path, $minProperties, $minProperties === 1 ? 'property' : 'properties');
            }

            $maxProperties = self::toIntConstraint($schema['maxProperties'] ?? null);
            if ($maxProperties !== null && $countsProperties && $count > $maxProperties) {
                $errors[] = sprintf('%s must have at most %d %s', $path, $maxProperties, $maxProperties === 1 ? 'property' : 'properties');
            }

            if ($isList && ($schema['uniqueItems'] ?? false) === true) {
                $seen = [];
                foreach ($valueArray as $item) {
                    $fingerprint = self::jsonValueFingerprint($item);
                    if ($fingerprint === null) {
                        $errors[] = sprintf('%s: JSON equality traversal exceeds its limits', $path);
                        break;
                    }
                    if (array_key_exists($fingerprint, $seen)) {
                        $errors[] = sprintf('%s must contain unique items', $path);
                        break;
                    }
                    $seen[$fingerprint] = true;
                }
            }
        }

        if (is_array($structuredValue) && is_array($schema['required'] ?? null)) {
            foreach ($schema['required'] as $requiredProp) {
                if (is_string($requiredProp) && !array_key_exists($requiredProp, $structuredValue)) {
                    $errors[] = sprintf('%s is required', self::openApiChildPath($path, $requiredProp));
                }
            }
        }

        if (is_array($schema['properties'] ?? null) && is_array($structuredValue)) {
            foreach ($schema['properties'] as $propertyName => $propertySchema) {
                // A property named by digits arrives as an int key; it is still a name.
                $propertyName = (string)$propertyName;
                if (!is_array($propertySchema)) {
                    continue;
                }
                if (!array_key_exists($propertyName, $structuredValue)) {
                    continue;
                }
                $errors = [
                    ...$errors,
                    ...self::validateOpenApiNode($structuredValue[$propertyName], $propertySchema, self::openApiChildPath($path, $propertyName), $depth + 1),
                ];
            }
        }

        if (is_array($schema['dependentRequired'] ?? null) && is_array($structuredValue)) {
            foreach ($schema['dependentRequired'] as $propertyName => $deps) {
                $propertyName = (string)$propertyName;
                if (!is_array($deps) || !array_key_exists($propertyName, $structuredValue)) {
                    continue;
                }
                foreach ($deps as $depProperty) {
                    if (is_string($depProperty) && !array_key_exists($depProperty, $structuredValue)) {
                        $errors[] = sprintf('%s is required when %s is present', self::openApiChildPath($path, $depProperty), $propertyName);
                    }
                }
            }
        }

        if (is_array($schema['not'] ?? null) && self::matchesOpenApiNode($value, $schema['not'], $path, $depth + 1)) {
            // `not: {}` is how the generator spells the schema `false`, and a document that wrote `false`
            // never mentioned `not` — the same sentence the runtime validator writes for it.
            $errors[] = $schema['not'] === []
                ? sprintf('%s is not allowed by the schema', $path)
                : sprintf('%s must not match the \'not\' schema', $path);
        }

        if (is_array($schema['if'] ?? null)) {
            if (self::matchesOpenApiNode($value, $schema['if'], $path, $depth + 1)) {
                if (is_array($schema['then'] ?? null)) {
                    $errors = [...$errors, ...self::validateOpenApiNode($value, $schema['then'], $path, $depth + 1)];
                }
            } elseif (is_array($schema['else'] ?? null)) {
                $errors = [...$errors, ...self::validateOpenApiNode($value, $schema['else'], $path, $depth + 1)];
            }
        }

        return $errors;
    }

    /**
     * Whether a subschema matches; its errors are thrown away. The path is not: an unfinished check —
     * the depth limit, a pattern the engine gave up on — is thrown out of here with it, and is the
     * refusal the caller ends up reporting, so it has to name the value it could not finish.
     *
     * @param array<string, mixed> $schema
     */
    private static function matchesOpenApiNode(mixed $value, array $schema, string $path, int $depth): bool
    {
        return self::validateOpenApiNode($value, $schema, $path, $depth) === [];
    }

    private static function matchesOpenApiCallbackType(mixed $value, mixed $type): bool
    {
        if (!is_string($type) || $type === '') {
            return true;
        }

        return match ($type) {
            'string' => is_string($value),
            default => true,
        };
    }

    private static function normalizeOpenApiStructuralValue(mixed $value): mixed
    {
        if (is_array($value) || !is_object($value)) {
            return $value;
        }

        // Any other object: only its public state is visible, under PHP property names.
        return get_object_vars($value);
    }

    private static function openApiChildPath(string $path, string $propertyName): string
    {
        // The first level names the field the way the runtime validator's caller does, so both
        // modes report the same subject: `field "tags" must ...`.
        return $path === 'payload'
            ? sprintf('field "%s"', $propertyName)
            : $path . '.' . $propertyName;
    }

    /**
     * @param array<int, string> $keys
     */
    private static function schemaHasAnyOpenApiKey(array $schema, array $keys): bool
    {
        foreach ($keys as $key) {
            if (array_key_exists($key, $schema)) {
                return true;
            }
        }

        return false;
    }

    private static function toIntConstraint(mixed $value): ?int
    {
        if (is_int($value)) {
            return $value;
        }
        if (is_float($value) && floor($value) === $value) {
            return (int)$value;
        }

        return null;
    }
}

<?php

declare(strict_types=1);

namespace GoldenCorpus;

use Closure;
use Illuminate\Contracts\Validation\ValidationRule;
use Illuminate\Contracts\Validation\ValidatorAwareRule;

/**
 * NOTE: This DTO is auto-generated from an OpenAPI schema.
 * Do not edit this file manually.
 *
 * laravel-data mode: one class, hydrated and validated by `spatie/laravel-data`.
 *
 *     $dto = Pet::from($request);
 *
 * Validation runs on that call because `validation_strategy` defaults to `OnlyRequests`. Given a plain
 * array instead, use `Pet::validateAndCreate($payload)` — `from($array)` hydrates WITHOUT
 * validating.
 */
final class Pet extends Data
{
    public function __construct(
        public readonly string $name,
    ) {
    }

    /**
     * Validation rules for the framework's validator, keyed by the OpenAPI property names — which is
     * what the request payload carries, not the PHP property names.
     *
     * Mentioning a property here takes it away from laravel-data's rule inferrers, which is the point:
     * these rules are the schema, theirs are a guess from the PHP type.
     *
     * @return array<string, array<int, mixed>>
     */
    public static function rules(): array
    {
        return [
            'name' => self::withBlankStringValidation(['present', 'string']),
        ];
    }

    /**
     * Laravel skips ordinary rules for blank strings. OpenAPI treats them as present values, so an
     * implicit rule checks only that skipped case, using the same native rules and translator.
     *
     * @param array<int, mixed> $rules
     * @return array<int, mixed>
     */
    private static function withBlankStringValidation(array $rules): array
    {
        $rules[] = new class($rules) implements ValidationRule, ValidatorAwareRule {
            /** Runs for a blank string too, which Laravel skips for an ordinary rule. */
            public bool $implicit = true;

            private ?Validator $validator = null;

            /** @param array<int, mixed> $rules */
            public function __construct(private readonly array $rules)
            {
            }

            public function setValidator(Validator $validator): static
            {
                $this->validator = $validator;

                return $this;
            }

            public function validate(string $attribute, mixed $value, Closure $fail): void
            {
                if (!is_string($value) || trim($value) !== '') {
                    return;
                }
                if ($this->validator === null) {
                    throw new LogicException('The blank-string rule requires a validator.');
                }

                $validator = new class(
                    translator: $this->validator->getTranslator(),
                    data: ['value' => $value],
                    rules: ['value' => $this->rules],
                    attributes: ['value' => $this->validator->getDisplayableAttribute($attribute)],
                ) extends Validator {
                    /**
                     * @param object|string $rule
                     * @param string $attribute
                     * @param mixed $value
                     */
                    protected function presentOrRuleIsImplicit($rule, $attribute, $value): bool
                    {
                        return $this->validatePresent($attribute, $value) || $this->isImplicit($rule);
                    }
                };
                if ($validator->passes()) {
                    return;
                }
                foreach ($validator->errors()->all() as $message) {
                    $fail($message);
                }
            }

            public function __toString(): string
            {
                return 'openapi_blank_string';
            }
        };

        return $rules;
    }

===== ReadOnlyFields.php =====
<?php

declare(strict_types=1);

namespace GoldenCorpus;

use Closure;
use DateTimeImmutable;
use Illuminate\Contracts\Validation\ValidationRule;
use Illuminate\Contracts\Validation\ValidatorAwareRule;
use Illuminate\Validation\Validator;
use LogicException;
use OverflowException;
use Spatie\LaravelData\Attributes\Hidden;
use Spatie\LaravelData\Attributes\WithCast;
use Spatie\LaravelData\Casts\DateTimeInterfaceCast;
use Spatie\LaravelData\Data;
use Spatie\LaravelData\Optional;

use function array_key_exists;
use function array_keys;
use function array_map;
use function array_shift;
use function array_slice;
use function implode;
use function is_array;
use function is_bool;
use function is_finite;
use function is_float;
use function is_string;
use function preg_match;
use function preg_match_all;
use function preg_split;
use function str_ends_with;
use function str_replace;
use function str_starts_with;
use function strlen;
use function substr;
use function trim;

/**
 * NOTE: This DTO is auto-generated from an OpenAPI schema.
 * Do not edit this file manually.
 *
 * laravel-data mode: one class, hydrated and validated by `spatie/laravel-data`.
 *
 *     $dto = ReadOnlyFields::from($request);
 *
 * Validation runs on that call because `validation_strategy` defaults to `OnlyRequests`. Given a plain
 * array instead, use `ReadOnlyFields::validateAndCreate($payload)` — `from($array)` hydrates WITHOUT
 * validating.
 *
 * An optional property is typed `…|Optional`, so an unprovided one IS a `Spatie\LaravelData\Optional`
 * and is omitted from `toArray()`. That keeps "absent" and "sent as null" apart without any bookkeeping
 * of ours — PATCH semantics as a language-level fact:
 *
 *     if (! $dto->serverId instanceof Optional) { … }
 */
final class ReadOnlyFields extends Data
{
    private const array OPENAPI_VALIDATION_CONSTRAINTS = [
        'fields' => [
            'serverId' => [
                'readOnly' => true,
            ],
            'writtenAt' => [
                'readOnly' => true,
            ],
        ],
        'object' => [],
    ];

    private const int OPENAPI_MAX_VALIDATION_DEPTH = 256;

    public readonly int|Optional $serverId;

    public readonly string $title;

    #[WithCast(DateTimeInterfaceCast::class, format: ['Y-m-d\TH:i:sP', 'Y-m-d\TH:i:s.uP', 'Y-m-d\TH:i:sp', 'Y-m-d\TH:i:s.up', 'Y-m-d H:i:s', 'Y-m-d\TH:i:s'])]
    public readonly DateTimeImmutable|Optional $writtenAt;

    #[Hidden]
    public readonly string|Optional $secret;

    public function __construct(
        string $title,
        int|Optional $serverId = new Optional(),
        DateTimeImmutable|Optional $writtenAt = new Optional(),
        string|Optional $secret = new Optional(),
    ) {
        $this->serverId = $serverId;
        $this->title = $title;
        $this->writtenAt = $writtenAt;
        $this->secret = $secret;
    }

    /**
     * Validation rules for the framework's validator, keyed by the OpenAPI property names — which is
     * what the request payload carries, not the PHP property names.
     *
     * Mentioning a property here takes it away from laravel-data's rule inferrers, which is the point:
     * these rules are the schema, theirs are a guess from the PHP type.
     *
     * @return array<string, array<int, mixed>>
     */
    public static function rules(): array
    {
        return [
            'serverId' => self::withBlankStringValidation(['sometimes', 'integer']),
            'title' => self::withBlankStringValidation(['present', 'string']),
            'writtenAt' => self::withBlankStringValidation(['sometimes', 'date_format:Y-m-d\TH:i:sP,Y-m-d\TH:i:s.uP,Y-m-d\TH:i:s.vP,Y-m-d\TH:i:sp,Y-m-d\TH:i:s.up,Y-m-d\TH:i:s.vp,Y-m-d H:i:s,Y-m-d\TH:i:s']),
            'secret' => self::withBlankStringValidation(['sometimes', 'string']),
        ];
    }

    /**
     * Laravel skips ordinary rules for blank strings. OpenAPI treats them as present values, so an
     * implicit rule checks only that skipped case, using the same native rules and translator.
     *
     * @param array<int, mixed> $rules
     * @return array<int, mixed>
     */
    private static function withBlankStringValidation(array $rules): array
    {
        $rules[] = new class($rules) implements ValidationRule, ValidatorAwareRule {
            /** Runs for a blank string too, which Laravel skips for an ordinary rule. */
            public bool $implicit = true;

            private ?Validator $validator = null;

            /** @param array<int, mixed> $rules */
            public function __construct(private readonly array $rules)
            {
            }

            public function setValidator(Validator $validator): static
            {
                $this->validator = $validator;

                return $this;
            }

            public function validate(string $attribute, mixed $value, Closure $fail): void
            {
                if (!is_string($value) || trim($value) !== '') {
                    return;
                }
                if ($this->validator === null) {
                    throw new LogicException('The blank-string rule requires a validator.');
                }

                $validator = new class(
                    translator: $this->validator->getTranslator(),
                    data: ['value' => $value],
                    rules: ['value' => $this->rules],
                    attributes: ['value' => $this->validator->getDisplayableAttribute($attribute)],
                ) extends Validator {
                    /**
                     * @param object|string $rule
                     * @param string $attribute
                     * @param mixed $value
                     */
                    protected function presentOrRuleIsImplicit($rule, $attribute, $value): bool
                    {
                        return $this->validatePresent($attribute, $value) || $this->isImplicit($rule);
                    }
                };
                if ($validator->passes()) {
                    return;
                }
                foreach ($validator->errors()->all() as $message) {
                    $fail($message);
                }
            }

            public function __toString(): string
            {
                return 'openapi_blank_string';
            }
        };

        return $rules;
    }

    /**
     * Paths whose schema is `integer` or `number`, with that type. A `*` segment matches every index of
     * an array and every key of a map.
     *
     * @var array<string, string>
     */
    private const array OPENAPI_SCALAR_PATHS = [
        'serverId' => 'integer',
    ];

    /**
     * The half of the schema Laravel's rule vocabulary cannot express — composition, conditionals,
     * `contains`, `unevaluated*`, `content*`, `propertyNames`, `discriminator` — checked against the
     * raw payload the validator already holds.
     *
     * laravel-data calls this itself, and only on the ROOT object: a nested `Data` class never gets its
     * own `withValidator()` run, which is why every check above is flattened to a path from the root.
     */
    public static function withValidator(Validator $validator): void
    {
        $validator->after(static function (Validator $validator): void {
            // `getData()` holds every `.` and `*` of a payload KEY as a placeholder; the schema and the
            // client name them literally, and an error under a dotted map key was reported at a random
            // `__dot__…` path. Laravel mode undoes them the same way.
            /** @var array<string, mixed> $payload */
            $payload = (fn(): array => $this->replacePlaceholders($this->getData()))->call($validator);

            foreach (self::OPENAPI_VALIDATION_CONSTRAINTS['fields'] as $property => $schema) {
                if (!array_key_exists($property, $payload)) {
                    continue;
                }

                foreach (self::validateOpenApiNode($payload[$property], $schema, $property, 0) as $error) {
                    self::addOpenApiFieldError($validator, $property, $payload[$property], $error);
                }
            }

            self::assertNoBooleanNumbers($validator);
        });
    }

    /**
     * Adds an error for every integer or number path that holds a boolean. Laravel's `integer` rule reads
     * `true` through `filter_var()` as 1, so it passed, and laravel-data then cast it to 1 or stored it
     * as `true` in an `array<int>`. Read from the validator's data, not the raw body: a boolean is still
     * a boolean once decoded, so `validateAndCreate($array)` is covered too. A path a rule already
     * failed is left alone, so one mistake is reported once.
     */
    private static function assertNoBooleanNumbers(Validator $validator): void
    {
        /** @var array<array-key, mixed> $data */
        $data = (fn(): array => $this->replacePlaceholders($this->getData()))->call($validator);

        foreach (self::OPENAPI_SCALAR_PATHS as $path => $type) {
            // A nested class reports its boolean paths too; a boolean there is what the schema asks for.
            if ($type === 'boolean') {
                continue;
            }

            // `\.` is a dot INSIDE a property name, as in `rules()`: it splits nothing.
            $segments = str_replace('\\.', '.', preg_split('/(?<!\\\\)\\./', $path) ?: []);
            foreach (self::dataNodesAt($data, $segments, '') as $foundPath => $node) {
                // A number past the float range (`1e400`) decodes to INF, which `numeric` takes and no
                // JSON encoder can write back.
                if ((is_bool($node) || (is_float($node) && !is_finite($node))) && !$validator->errors()->has($foundPath)) {
                    $validator->addFailure($foundPath, $type === 'number' ? 'numeric' : 'integer');
                }
            }
        }
    }

    /**
     * Every value a (possibly wildcarded) path reaches in decoded data, keyed by the concrete path.
     *
     * @param array<int, string> $segments
     * @return array<string, mixed>
     */
    private static function dataNodesAt(mixed $node, array $segments, string $path): array
    {
        if ($segments === []) {
            return [$path => $node];
        }
        if (!is_array($node)) {
            return [];
        }

        $segment = array_shift($segments);
        // `*` stands for both an array index and a map key.
        $keys = $segment === '*' ? array_keys($node) : (array_key_exists($segment, $node) ? [$segment] : []);
        $found = [];
        foreach ($keys as $key) {
            $keyPath = $path === '' ? (string)$key : $path . '.' . $key;
            foreach (self::dataNodesAt($node[$key], $segments, $keyPath) as $deepPath => $deepNode) {
                $found[$deepPath] = $deepNode;
            }
        }

        return $found;
    }

    /**
     * Reports an interpreter message under the field it is about, as Laravel's own rules do: a key
     * missing deep inside a union lands on `shape.radius`, a list item on `levels.0` — the message
     * opens with that path. Before 2.15.91 every one of them was keyed by the top-level property.
     *
     * A claim a Laravel rule also makes — a missing key, a wrong type, a value outside an enum — is
     * reported AS that rule, so it reads like the rest of the error bag and the application's
     * translations apply. Anything else keeps the interpreter's sentence.
     *
     * A path the payload does not lead along — one into a JSON string's decoded `contentSchema` — is not
     * a field of the request, so that message stays on the property with its own sentence.
     */
    private static function addOpenApiFieldError(Validator $validator, string $property, mixed $value, string $error): void
    {
        // `shape.radius is required`, `levels[0] must be one of: 1, 2`: the deeper segments, then the claim.
        $rest = str_starts_with($error, $property) ? substr($error, strlen($property)) : '';
        if (preg_match('/^((?:\.[^ .\[]+|\[\d+\])*) (.+)$/s', $rest, $match) !== 1) {
            $validator->errors()->add($property, str_ends_with($error, '.') ? $error : $error . '.');

            return;
        }

        preg_match_all('/\.([^ .\[]+)|\[(\d+)\]/', $match[1], $found, PREG_SET_ORDER);
        $segments = array_map(static fn(array $segment): string => ($segment[2] ?? '') !== '' ? $segment[2] : $segment[1], $found);
        $node = $value;
        foreach (array_slice($segments, 0, -1) as $segment) {
            if (!is_array($node) || !array_key_exists($segment, $node)) {
                $node = null;
                break;
            }
            $node = $node[$segment];
        }
        if ($segments !== [] && !is_array($node)) {
            $validator->errors()->add($property, str_ends_with($error, '.') ? $error : $error . '.');

            return;
        }

        $path = implode('.', [$property, ...$segments]);
        $claim = $match[2];
        // A field a rule has already failed — `kind` of a discriminated value carries `Rule::enum()`,
        // a union that holds a scalar branch carries `array` — is one mistake, reported once.
        if ($validator->errors()->has($path) && ($segments !== [] || str_starts_with($claim, 'does not match any '))) {
            return;
        }
        // The same for a union whose member's rules already failed below it: `null` for a discriminated
        // union is `pet.kind` required, and "pet does not match any oneOf branch" beside it said it twice.
        if (str_starts_with($claim, 'does not match any ') && $validator->errors()->has($path . '.*')) {
            return;
        }

        $rule = match ($claim) {
            'is required' => 'present',
            'must be of type integer' => 'integer',
            'must be of type number' => 'numeric',
            'must be of type string' => 'string',
            'must be of type boolean' => 'boolean',
            'must be of type array' => 'list',
            'must be of type object' => 'array',
            default => str_starts_with($claim, 'must be one of: ') ? 'enum' : null,
        };

        if ($rule !== null) {
            $validator->addFailure($path, $rule);

            return;
        }

        $validator->errors()->add($path, str_ends_with($error, '.') ? $error : $error . '.');
    }

    /**
     * @param array<string, mixed> $schema
     * @return array<int, string>
     */
    private static function validateOpenApiNode(mixed $value, array $schema, string $path, int $depth): array
    {
        if ($depth > 0) {
            return self::validateOpenApiNodeUnchecked($value, $schema, $path, $depth);
        }
        try {
            return self::validateOpenApiNodeUnchecked($value, $schema, $path, $depth);
        } catch (OverflowException $error) {
            return [$error->getMessage()];
        }
    }

    /**
     * @param array<string, mixed> $schema
     * @return array<int, string>
     */
    private static function validateOpenApiNodeUnchecked(mixed $value, array $schema, string $path, int $depth): array
    {
        // An incomplete check is not a branch mismatch: not/if/anyOf must not invert or swallow it.
        if ($depth >= self::OPENAPI_MAX_VALIDATION_DEPTH) {
            throw new OverflowException($path . ' exceeds maximum validation depth.');
        }

        $errors = [];

        return $errors;
    }
}

    /**
     * Validation rules for the framework's validator, keyed by the OpenAPI property names — which is
     * what the request payload carries, not the PHP property names.
     *
     * Mentioning a property here takes it away from laravel-data's rule inferrers, which is the point:
     * these rules are the schema, theirs are a guess from the PHP type.
     *
     * @return array<string, array<int, mixed>>
     */

    {
        return [
            'legacyId' => self::withBlankStringValidation(['present', 'integer']),
        ];
    }

    /**
     * Laravel skips ordinary rules for blank strings. OpenAPI treats them as present values, so an
     * implicit rule checks only that skipped case, using the same native rules and translator.
     *
     * @param array<int, mixed> $rules
     * @return array<int, mixed>
     */
    private static function withBlankStringValidation(array $rules): array
    {
        $rules[] = new class($rules) implements ValidationRule, ValidatorAwareRule {
            /** Runs for a blank string too, which Laravel skips for an ordinary rule. */
            public bool $implicit = true;

            private ?Validator $validator = null;

            /** @param array<int, mixed> $rules */
            public function __construct(private readonly array $rules)
            {
            }

            public function setValidator(Validator $validator): static
            {
                $this->validator = $validator;

                return $this;
            }

            public function validate(string $attribute, mixed $value, Closure $fail): void
            {
                if (!is_string($value) || trim($value) !== '') {
                    return;
                }
                if ($this->validator === null) {
                    throw new LogicException('The blank-string rule requires a validator.');
                }

                $validator = new class(
                    translator: $this->validator->getTranslator(),
                    data: ['value' => $value],
                    rules: ['value' => $this->rules],
                    attributes: ['value' => $this->validator->getDisplayableAttribute($attribute)],
                ) extends Validator {
                    /**
                     * @param object|string $rule
                     * @param string $attribute
                     * @param mixed $value
                     */
                    protected function presentOrRuleIsImplicit($rule, $attribute, $value): bool
                    {
                        return $this->validatePresent($attribute, $value) || $this->isImplicit($rule);
                    }
                };
                if ($validator->passes()) {
                    return;
                }
                foreach ($validator->errors()->all() as $message) {
                    $fail($message);
                }
            }

            public function __toString(): string
            {
                return 'openapi_blank_string';
            }
        };

        return $rules;
    }

    /**
     * Paths whose schema is `integer` or `number`, with that type. A `*` segment matches every index of
     * an array and every key of a map.
     *
     * @var array<string, string>
     */
    private const array OPENAPI_SCALAR_PATHS = [
        'legacyId' => 'integer',
    ];

    /**
     * The one value Laravel's rules cannot refuse: a JSON boolean where the schema asks for a number.
     *
     * laravel-data calls this itself, and only on the ROOT object: a nested `Data` class never gets its
     * own `withValidator()` run, which is why every check above is flattened to a path from the root.
     */
    public static function withValidator(Validator $validator): void
    {
        $validator->after(static function (Validator $validator): void {
            self::assertNoBooleanNumbers($validator);
        });
    }

    /**
     * Adds an error for every integer or number path that holds a boolean. Laravel's `integer` rule reads
     * `true` through `filter_var()` as 1, so it passed, and laravel-data then cast it to 1 or stored it
     * as `true` in an `array<int>`. Read from the validator's data, not the raw body: a boolean is still
     * a boolean once decoded, so `validateAndCreate($array)` is covered too. A path a rule already
     * failed is left alone, so one mistake is reported once.
     */
    private static function assertNoBooleanNumbers(Validator $validator): void
    {
        /** @var array<array-key, mixed> $data */
        $data = (fn(): array => $this->replacePlaceholders($this->getData()))->call($validator);

        foreach (self::OPENAPI_SCALAR_PATHS as $path => $type) {
            // A nested class reports its boolean paths too; a boolean there is what the schema asks for.
            if ($type === 'boolean') {
                continue;
            }

            // `\.` is a dot INSIDE a property name, as in `rules()`: it splits nothing.
            $segments = str_replace('\\.', '.', preg_split('/(?<!\\\\)\\./', $path) ?: []);
            foreach (self::dataNodesAt($data, $segments, '') as $foundPath => $node) {
                // A number past the float range (`1e400`) decodes to INF, which `numeric` takes and no
                // JSON encoder can write back.
                if ((is_bool($node) || (is_float($node) && !is_finite($node))) && !$validator->errors()->has($foundPath)) {
                    $validator->addFailure($foundPath, $type === 'number' ? 'numeric' : 'integer');
                }
            }
        }
    }

    /**
     * Every value a (possibly wildcarded) path reaches in decoded data, keyed by the concrete path.
     *
     * @param array<int, string> $segments
     * @return array<string, mixed>
     */
    private static function dataNodesAt(mixed $node, array $segments, string $path): array
    {
        if ($segments === []) {
            return [$path => $node];
        }
        if (!is_array($node)) {
            return [];
        }

        $segment = array_shift($segments);
        // `*` stands for both an array index and a map key.
        $keys = $segment === '*' ? array_keys($node) : (array_key_exists($segment, $node) ? [$segment] : []);
        $found = [];

===== Shape.php =====
<?php

declare(strict_types=1);

namespace GoldenCorpus;

use Illuminate\Contracts\Support\Arrayable;
use Illuminate\Http\Request;
use Illuminate\Support\Facades\Validator;
use Illuminate\Validation\Rule;
use Spatie\LaravelData\Attributes\PropertyForMorph;
use Spatie\LaravelData\Contracts\PropertyMorphableData;
use Spatie\LaravelData\Data;

use function get_debug_type;
use function is_array;
use function is_float;
use function is_int;
use function is_string;
use function sprintf;

/**
 * NOTE: This DTO is auto-generated from an OpenAPI schema.
 * Do not edit this file manually.
 *
 * laravel-data mode: the abstract base of a discriminated union. It is never built directly — a payload
 * naming one of the members is hydrated into that member, chosen by `morph()` from
 * `kind`. The other modes emit an interface here; laravel-data cannot hydrate one,
 * and `PropertyMorphableData` is its own answer to the same question.
 */
abstract class Shape extends Data implements PropertyMorphableData
{
    public function __construct(
        #[PropertyForMorph]
        public readonly string $kind,
    ) {
    }

    /**
     * Which member class a payload describes, read from the discriminator BEFORE anything is hydrated —
     * which is why laravel-data needs a method rather than the type system: the value decides the class.
     *
     * @param array<string, mixed> $properties
     */
    public static function morph(array $properties): ?string
    {
        return match ($properties['kind'] ?? null) {
            'circle' => Circle::class,
            'square' => Square::class,
            // An unmapped value leaves the class unresolved, and laravel-data refuses to build the
            // abstract base — the discriminator is enforced by the schema's own rules besides.
            default => null,
        };
    }

    /**
     * `from()` with the discriminator checked first. laravel-data picks the member class before it
     * validates anything, so an unknown or missing value was `CannotCreateAbstractClass` — a 500 — where
     * the same union nested in another class answers 422. Checked here, it is a 422 on
     * `kind` like any other invalid field.
     */
    public static function from(mixed ...$payloads): static
    {
        foreach ($payloads as $payload) {
            self::assertKnownMember($payload);
        }

        return parent::from(...$payloads);
    }

    /**
     * @param Arrayable<array-key, mixed>|array<array-key, mixed> $payload
     */
    public static function validateAndCreate(Arrayable|array $payload): static
    {
        self::assertKnownMember($payload);

        return parent::validateAndCreate($payload);
    }

    private static function assertKnownMember(mixed $payload): void
    {
        $values = match (true) {
            $payload instanceof Request => $payload->all(),
            $payload instanceof Arrayable => $payload->toArray(),
            is_array($payload) => $payload,
            // Anything else — a model, an object — is laravel-data's to read.
            default => null,
        };
        if ($values === null) {
            return;
        }

        Validator::make(
            ['kind' => $values['kind'] ?? null],
            ['kind' => ['required', Rule::in(['circle', 'square'])]],
        )->validate();
    }

    /**
     * What is wrong with the value a holder found where this union is expected, keyed by path. laravel-data
     * reads the discriminator out of that value before any rule runs, and a string or a number there was
     * a `TypeError` from `array_key_exists()` — a 500 — where a plain object property answers 422. A
     * property's null is read as an empty payload and left to the rules; a list item or map value is
     * read as it is, null included. The discriminator itself is read the same way, by its wire name and
     * by the property's, and anything but a string or a number there — an object, a list, a boolean —
     * broke laravel-data's own `DataMorphClassResolver::normalizeValue()` with a `TypeError`.
     *
     * @internal called by the classes that hold this union
     * @return array<string, string>
     */
    public static function openApiMorphUnionErrors(mixed $value, string $path, bool $isItem): array
    {
        if (!is_array($value)) {
            return $value === null && !$isItem ? [] : [$path => sprintf('%s expects object, got %s.', $path, get_debug_type($value))];
        }
        $discriminator = $value['kind'] ?? null;
        if ($discriminator !== null && !is_string($discriminator) && !is_int($discriminator) && !is_float($discriminator)) {
            return [$path . '.kind' => sprintf('%s.kind expects string, got %s.', $path, get_debug_type($discriminator))];
        }

        return [];
    }
}

===== Tag.php =====
<?php

 * NOTE: This DTO is auto-generated from an OpenAPI schema.
 * Do not edit this file manually.

 * laravel-data mode: one class, hydrated and validated by `spatie/laravel-data`.

 *     $dto = Tag::from($request);

     * Validation rules for the framework's validator, keyed by the OpenAPI property names — which is
     * what the request payload carries, not the PHP property names.
     *
     * Mentioning a property here takes it away from laravel-data's rule inferrers, which is the point:
     * these rules are the schema, theirs are a guess from the PHP type.
     *
     * @return array<string, array<int, mixed>>
     */
    public static function rules(): array
    {
        return [
            'id' => self::withBlankStringValidation(['present', 'integer']),
            'name' => self::withBlankStringValidation(['present', 'string']),
        ];
    }

    /**
     * Laravel skips ordinary rules for blank strings. OpenAPI treats them as present values, so an
     * implicit rule checks only that skipped case, using the same native rules and translator.
     *
     * @param array<int, mixed> $rules
     * @return array<int, mixed>
     */
    private static function withBlankStringValidation(array $rules): array
    {
        $rules[] = new class($rules) implements ValidationRule, ValidatorAwareRule {
            /** Runs for a blank string too, which Laravel skips for an ordinary rule. */
            public bool $implicit = true;

            private ?Validator $validator = null;

            /** @param array<int, mixed> $rules */
            public function __construct(private readonly array $rules)
            {
            }

            public function setValidator(Validator $validator): static
            {
                $this->validator = $validator;

                return $this;
            }

            public function validate(string $attribute, mixed $value, Closure $fail): void
            {
                if (!is_string($value) || trim($value) !== '') {
                    return;
                }
                if ($this->validator === null) {
                    throw new LogicException('The blank-string rule requires a validator.');
                }

                $validator = new class(
                    translator: $this->validator->getTranslator(),
                    data: ['value' => $value],
                    rules: ['value' => $this->rules],
                    attributes: ['value' => $this->validator->getDisplayableAttribute($attribute)],
                ) extends Validator {
                    /**
                     * @param object|string $rule
                     * @param string $attribute
                     * @param mixed $value
                     */
                    protected function presentOrRuleIsImplicit($rule, $attribute, $value): bool
                    {
                        return $this->validatePresent($attribute, $value) || $this->isImplicit($rule);
                    }
                };
                if ($validator->passes()) {
                    return;
                }
                foreach ($validator->errors()->all() as $message) {
                    $fail($message);
                }
            }

            public function __toString(): string
            {
                return 'openapi_blank_string';
            }
        };

        return $rules;
    }

    /**
     * What this class adds to the validation of a payload that holds it at `$prefix`: its dotted rules —
     * presence left out, no rule can say "required only if the parent has a value" — and the paths whose
     * wire shape is checked against the raw body. A parent asks for it instead of repeating it under
     * every path that reaches this class.
     *
     * @internal called by the generated classes that hold this one
     * @param array<int, string> $visited the classes above this one; one already among them adds nothing
     * @return array{rules: array<string, array<int, mixed>>, objectPaths: array<int, string>, scalarPaths: array<string, string>}
     */
    public static function openApiNestedValidation(string $prefix, array $visited): array
    {
        if (in_array(self::class, $visited, true)) {
            return ['rules' => [], 'objectPaths' => [], 'scalarPaths' => []];
        }
        $visited[] = self::class;

        $parts = [
            [
                'rules' => [
                    $prefix . '.id' => self::withBlankStringValidation(['integer']),
                    $prefix . '.name' => self::withBlankStringValidation(['string']),
                ],
                'objectPaths' => [
                ],
                'scalarPaths' => [
                    $prefix . '.id' => 'integer',
                ],
            ],
        ];

        return [
            'rules' => array_merge(...array_column($parts, 'rules')),
            'objectPaths' => array_merge(...array_column($parts, 'objectPaths')),
            'scalarPaths' => array_merge(...array_column($parts, 'scalarPaths')),
        ];
    }

    /**
     * Paths whose schema is `integer` or `number`, with that type. A `*` segment matches every index of
     * an array and every key of a map.
     *
     * @var array<string, string>
     */
    private const array OPENAPI_SCALAR_PATHS = [
        'id' => 'integer',
    ];

    /**
     * The one value Laravel's rules cannot refuse: a JSON boolean where the schema asks for a number.
     *
     * laravel-data calls this itself, and only on the ROOT object: a nested `Data` class never gets its
     * own `withValidator()` run, which is why every check above is flattened to a path from the root.
     */
    public static function withValidator(Validator $validator): void
    {
        $validator->after(static function (Validator $validator): void {
            self::assertNoBooleanNumbers($validator);
        });
    }

    /**
     * Adds an error for every integer or number path that holds a boolean. Laravel's `integer` rule reads
     * `true` through `filter_var()` as 1, so it passed, and laravel-data then cast it to 1 or stored it
     * as `true` in an `array<int>`. Read from the validator's data, not the raw body: a boolean is still
     * a boolean once decoded, so `validateAndCreate($array)` is covered too. A path a rule already
     * failed is left alone, so one mistake is reported once.
     */
    private static function assertNoBooleanNumbers(Validator $validator): void
    {
        /** @var array<array-key, mixed> $data */
        $data = (fn(): array => $this->replacePlaceholders($this->getData()))->call($validator);

        foreach (self::OPENAPI_SCALAR_PATHS as $path => $type) {
            // A nested class reports its boolean paths too; a boolean there is what the schema asks for.
            if ($type === 'boolean') {
                continue;
            }

            // `\.` is a dot INSIDE a property name, as in `rules()`: it splits nothing.
            $segments = str_replace('\\.', '.', preg_split('/(?<!\\\\)\\./', $path) ?: []);
            foreach (self::dataNodesAt($data, $segments, '') as $foundPath => $node) {
                // A number past the float range (`1e400`) decodes to INF, which `numeric` takes and no
                // JSON encoder can write back.
                if ((is_bool($node) || (is_float($node) && !is_finite($node))) && !$validator->errors()->has($foundPath)) {
                    $validator->addFailure($foundPath, $type === 'number' ? 'numeric' : 'integer');
                }
            }
        }
    }

    /**
     * Every value a (possibly wildcarded) path reaches in decoded data, keyed by the concrete path.
     *
     * @param array<int, string> $segments
     * @return array<string, mixed>
     */
    private static function dataNodesAt(mixed $node, array $segments, string $path): array
    {
        if ($segments === []) {
            return [$path => $node];
        }
        if (!is_array($node)) {
            return [];
        }

        $segment = array_shift($segments);
        // `*` stands for both an array index and a map key.
        $keys = $segment === '*' ? array_keys($node) : (array_key_exists($segment, $node) ? [$segment] : []);
        $found = [];
        foreach ($keys as $key) {
            $keyPath = $path === '' ? (string)$key : $path . '.' . $key;
            foreach (self::dataNodesAt($node[$key], $segments, $keyPath) as $deepPath => $deepNode) {
                $found[$deepPath] = $deepNode;
            }
        }

        return $found;
    }
}

    /**
     * Paths whose schema is `type: object`. A `*` segment matches every index of an array.

    private const array OPENAPI_OBJECT_PATHS = [

    /**

     */
    private const array OPENAPI_SCALAR_PATHS = [
        'id' => 'integer',

    /**
     * The half of the schema Laravel's rule vocabulary cannot express — composition, conditionals,
     * `contains`, `unevaluated*`, `content*`, `propertyNames`, `discriminator` — checked against the
     * raw payload the validator already holds.
     *
     * laravel-data calls this itself, and only on the ROOT object: a nested `Data` class never gets its
     * own `withValidator()` run, which is why every check above is flattened to a path from the root.
     *
     * One check needs the UNDECODED body, because `type: object` versus `type: array` is a property of
     * the wire shape that PHP loses — a JSON object keyed 0..n-1 and a JSON array decode to the same
     * list. Only the request still has those bytes, so on a `validateAndCreate($array)` call, where
     * there is no request, that single check is skipped and everything else still runs.
     */
    public static function withValidator(Validator $validator): void
    {
        $validator->after(static function (Validator $validator): void {
            // `getData()` holds every `.` and `*` of a payload KEY as a placeholder; the schema and the
            // client name them literally, and an error under a dotted map key was reported at a random
            // `__dot__…` path. Laravel mode undoes them the same way.
            /** @var array<string, mixed> $payload */
            $payload = (fn(): array => $this->replacePlaceholders($this->getData()))->call($validator);

            foreach (self::OPENAPI_VALIDATION_CONSTRAINTS['fields'] as $property => $schema) {
                if (!array_key_exists($property, $payload)) {
                    continue;
                }

                foreach (self::validateOpenApiNode($payload[$property], $schema, $property, 0) as $error) {
                    self::addOpenApiFieldError($validator, $property, $payload[$property], $error);
                }
            }

            self::assertNoBooleanNumbers($validator);

            $rawJson = self::rawRequestBody($validator);
            if ($rawJson !== null && $rawJson !== '') {
                self::assertRawObjectShapes($validator, $rawJson);
            }
        });
    }

    /**
     * The undecoded request body — or null when there is no request, or when what the validator holds is
     * not that body. `validate($array)` on a response and `validateAndCreate()` on one item of a list run
     * this same method while a request is bound, and its body says nothing about their data: a request
     * `{"meta":[1]}` made a response with an object `meta` fail. `from($request)` validates the request's
     * input, which is the body merged with the query, so every key of the body is there unchanged.
     */
    private static function rawRequestBody(Validator $validator): ?string
    {
        $container = Container::getInstance();
        if (!$container->bound('request')) {
            return null;
        }

        $request = $container->make('request');
        if (!$request instanceof Request) {
            return null;
        }

        $content = $request->getContent();
        $body = json_decode($content, true);
        if (!is_array($body)) {
            return null;
        }

        // `getData()` holds every `.` and `*` of a key as a placeholder; the body spells them literally.
        /** @var array<array-key, mixed> $data */
        $data = (fn(): array => $this->replacePlaceholders($this->getData()))->call($validator);
        foreach ($body as $key => $value) {
            if (!array_key_exists($key, $data) || $data[$key] != $value) {
                return null;
            }
        }

        return $content;
    }

    /**
     * Adds an error for every integer or number path that holds a boolean. Laravel's `integer` rule reads
     * `true` through `filter_var()` as 1, so it passed, and laravel-data then cast it to 1 or stored it
     * as `true` in an `array<int>`. Read from the validator's data, not the raw body: a boolean is still
     * a boolean once decoded, so `validateAndCreate($array)` is covered too. A path a rule already
     * failed is left alone, so one mistake is reported once.
     */
    private static function assertNoBooleanNumbers(Validator $validator): void
    {
        /** @var array<array-key, mixed> $data */
        $data = (fn(): array => $this->replacePlaceholders($this->getData()))->call($validator);

        foreach ([...self::OPENAPI_SCALAR_PATHS, ...self::openApiNestedParts()['scalarPaths']] as $path => $type) {
            // A nested class reports its boolean paths too; a boolean there is what the schema asks for.
            if ($type === 'boolean') {
                continue;
            }

            // `\.` is a dot INSIDE a property name, as in `rules()`: it splits nothing.
            $segments = str_replace('\\.', '.', preg_split('/(?<!\\\\)\\./', $path) ?: []);
            foreach (self::dataNodesAt($data, $segments, '') as $foundPath => $node) {
                // A number past the float range (`1e400`) decodes to INF, which `numeric` takes and no
                // JSON encoder can write back.
                if ((is_bool($node) || (is_float($node) && !is_finite($node))) && !$validator->errors()->has($foundPath)) {
                    $validator->addFailure($foundPath, $type === 'number' ? 'numeric' : 'integer');
                }
            }
        }
    }

    /**
     * Every value a (possibly wildcarded) path reaches in decoded data, keyed by the concrete path.
     *
     * @param array<int, string> $segments
     * @return array<string, mixed>
     */
    private static function dataNodesAt(mixed $node, array $segments, string $path): array
    {
        if ($segments === []) {
            return [$path => $node];
        }
        if (!is_array($node)) {
            return [];
        }

        $segment = array_shift($segments);
        // `*` stands for both an array index and a map key.
        $keys = $segment === '*' ? array_keys($node) : (array_key_exists($segment, $node) ? [$segment] : []);
        $found = [];
        foreach ($keys as $key) {
            $keyPath = $path === '' ? (string)$key : $path . '.' . $key;
            foreach (self::dataNodesAt($node[$key], $segments, $keyPath) as $deepPath => $deepNode) {
                $found[$deepPath] = $deepNode;
            }
        }

        return $found;
    }

    /**
     * Adds an error for every `type: object` path the raw body sent as a JSON array.
     */
    private static function assertRawObjectShapes(Validator $validator, string $rawJson): void
    {
        // Decoded WITHOUT the assoc flag on purpose: that is what keeps a JSON object apart from a
        // JSON array. Laravel's own `$validator->getData()` cannot answer this question.
        $decoded = json_decode($rawJson, false);
        if (!$decoded instanceof stdClass) {
            return;
        }

        foreach ([...self::OPENAPI_OBJECT_PATHS, ...self::openApiNestedParts()['objectPaths']] as $path) {
            foreach (self::rawNodesAt($decoded, explode('.', $path), '') as $foundPath => $node) {
                // `[]` is how PHP encodes an empty array, so it is the empty object a PHP client sends.
                if (is_array($node) && $node !== []) {
                    $validator->errors()->add($foundPath, sprintf('%s expects object, got array.', $foundPath));
                }
            }
        }
    }

    /**
     * Every node a (possibly wildcarded) path reaches, keyed by the concrete path that got there.
     *
     * @param array<int, string> $segments
     * @return array<string, mixed>
     */
    private static function rawNodesAt(mixed $node, array $segments, string $path): array
    {
        if ($segments === []) {
            return [$path => $node];
        }

        $segment = array_shift($segments);
        if ($segment === '*') {
            // `*` stands for both an array index and a map key, so a JSON object node is walked too.
            if ($node instanceof stdClass) {
                $node = get_object_vars($node);
            }
            if (!is_array($node)) {
                return [];
            }

            $found = [];
            foreach ($node as $index => $item) {
                $itemPath = $path === '' ? (string)$index : $path . '.' . $index;
                foreach (self::rawNodesAt($item, $segments, $itemPath) as $deepPath => $deepNode) {
                    $found[$deepPath] = $deepNode;
                }
            }

            return $found;
        }

        if (!$node instanceof stdClass || !property_exists($node, $segment)) {
            return [];
        }

        return self::rawNodesAt($node->{$segment}, $segments, $path === '' ? $segment : $path . '.' . $segment);
    }

    /**
     * Reports an interpreter message under the field it is about, as Laravel's own rules do: a key
     * missing deep inside a union lands on `shape.radius`, a list item on `levels.0` — the message
     * opens with that path. Before 2.15.91 every one of them was keyed by the top-level property.
     *
     * A claim a Laravel rule also makes — a missing key, a wrong type, a value outside an enum — is
     * reported AS that rule, so it reads like the rest of the error bag and the application's
     * translations apply. Anything else keeps the interpreter's sentence.
     *
     * A path the payload does not lead along — one into a JSON string's decoded `contentSchema` — is not
     * a field of the request, so that message stays on the property with its own sentence.
     */
    private static function addOpenApiFieldError(Validator $validator, string $property, mixed $value, string $error): void
    {
        // `shape.radius is required`, `levels[0] must be one of: 1, 2`: the deeper segments, then the claim.
        $rest = str_starts_with($error, $property) ? substr($error, strlen($property)) : '';
        if (preg_match('/^((?:\.[^ .\[]+|\[\d+\])*) (.+)$/s', $rest, $match) !== 1) {
            $validator->errors()->add($property, str_ends_with($error, '.') ? $error : $error . '.');

            return;
        }

        preg_match_all('/\.([^ .\[]+)|\[(\d+)\]/', $match[1], $found, PREG_SET_ORDER);
        $segments = array_map(static fn(array $segment): string => ($segment[2] ?? '') !== '' ? $segment[2] : $segment[1], $found);
        $node = $value;
        foreach (array_slice($segments, 0, -1) as $segment) {
            if (!is_array($node) || !array_key_exists($segment, $node)) {
                $node = null;
                break;
            }
            $node = $node[$segment];
        }
        if ($segments !== [] && !is_array($node)) {
            $validator->errors()->add($property, str_ends_with($error, '.') ? $error : $error . '.');

            return;
        }

        $path = implode('.', [$property, ...$segments]);
        $claim = $match[2];
        // A field a rule has already failed — `kind` of a discriminated value carries `Rule::enum()`,
        // a union that holds a scalar branch carries `array` — is one mistake, reported once.
        if ($validator->errors()->has($path) && ($segments !== [] || str_starts_with($claim, 'does not match any '))) {
            return;
        }
        // The same for a union whose member's rules already failed below it: `null` for a discriminated
        // union is `pet.kind` required, and "pet does not match any oneOf branch" beside it said it twice.
        if (str_starts_with($claim, 'does not match any ') && $validator->errors()->has($path . '.*')) {
            return;
        }

        $rule = match ($claim) {
            'is required' => 'present',
            'must be of type integer' => 'integer',
            'must be of type number' => 'numeric',
            'must be of type string' => 'string',
            'must be of type boolean' => 'boolean',
            'must be of type array' => 'list',
            'must be of type object' => 'array',
            default => str_starts_with($claim, 'must be one of: ') ? 'enum' : null,
        };

        if ($rule !== null) {
            $validator->addFailure($path, $rule);

            return;
        }

        $validator->errors()->add($path, str_ends_with($error, '.') ? $error : $error . '.');
    }

    /**
     * @param array<string, mixed> $schema
     * @return array<int, string>
     */
    private static function validateOpenApiNode(mixed $value, array $schema, string $path, int $depth): array
    {
        if ($depth > 0) {
            return self::validateOpenApiNodeUnchecked($value, $schema, $path, $depth);
        }
        try {
            return self::validateOpenApiNodeUnchecked($value, $schema, $path, $depth);
        } catch (OverflowException $error) {
            return [$error->getMessage()];
        }
    }

    /**
     * @param array<string, mixed> $schema
     * @return array<int, string>
     */
    private static function validateOpenApiNodeUnchecked(mixed $value, array $schema, string $path, int $depth): array
    {
        // An incomplete check is not a branch mismatch: not/if/anyOf must not invert or swallow it.
        if ($depth >= self::OPENAPI_MAX_VALIDATION_DEPTH) {
            throw new OverflowException($path . ' exceeds maximum validation depth.');
        }

        $recursiveRef = $schema['x-openapi-recurse'] ?? null;
        if (is_string($recursiveRef) && array_key_exists($recursiveRef, self::OPENAPI_RECURSIVE_SCHEMAS)) {
            /** @var array<string, mixed> $resolved */
            $resolved = self::OPENAPI_RECURSIVE_SCHEMAS[$recursiveRef];
            $schema = [...$resolved, ...array_diff_key($schema, ['x-openapi-recurse' => true])];
        }
        $errors = [];
        $normalizedValue = $value;
        $structuredValue = self::normalizeOpenApiStructuralValue($value);
        if (
            is_array($structuredValue)
            && $structuredValue !== []
            && array_is_list($structuredValue)
            && (!in_array('object', (array)($schema['type'] ?? []), true) || in_array('array', (array)($schema['type'] ?? []), true))
        ) {
            $structuredValue = null;
        }

        if (array_key_exists('type', $schema) && !($value === null && ($schema['nullable'] ?? false) === true)) {
            $typeConstraint = $schema['type'];
            if (is_string($typeConstraint)) {
                if (!self::matchesOpenApiCallbackType($normalizedValue, $typeConstraint)) {
                    $errors[] = sprintf('%s must be of type %s', $path, $typeConstraint);
                }
            }
        }

        if (is_array($structuredValue) && is_array($schema['required'] ?? null)) {
            foreach ($schema['required'] as $requiredProp) {
                if (is_string($requiredProp) && !array_key_exists($requiredProp, $structuredValue)) {
                    $errors[] = sprintf('%s is required', self::openApiChildPath($path, $requiredProp));
                }
            }
        }

        if (is_array($schema['properties'] ?? null) && is_array($structuredValue)) {
            foreach ($schema['properties'] as $propertyName => $propertySchema) {
                // A property named by digits arrives as an int key; it is still a name.
                $propertyName = (string)$propertyName;
                if (!is_array($propertySchema)) {
                    continue;
                }
                if (!array_key_exists($propertyName, $structuredValue)) {
                    continue;
                }
                $errors = [
                    ...$errors,
                    ...self::validateOpenApiNode($structuredValue[$propertyName], $propertySchema, self::openApiChildPath($path, $propertyName), $depth + 1),
                ];
            }
        }

        $listValue = is_array($value) && array_is_list($value);

        if ($listValue && ($schema['items'] ?? null) === false) {
            foreach (array_keys($value) as $index) {
                $errors[] = sprintf('%s[%s] is not allowed by the schema', $path, $index);
            }
        }

        if ($listValue && is_array($schema['items'] ?? null)) {
            foreach ($value as $index => $itemValue) {
                $errors = [
                    ...$errors,
                    ...self::validateOpenApiNode($itemValue, $schema['items'], $path . '[' . $index . ']', $depth + 1),
                ];
            }
        }

        if (is_array($schema['allOf'] ?? null)) {
            foreach ($schema['allOf'] as $branch) {
                if (!is_array($branch)) {
                    continue;
                }

                $errors = [...$errors, ...self::validateOpenApiNode($value, $branch, $path, $depth + 1)];
            }
        }

        if (is_array($schema['anyOf'] ?? null)) {
            $anyOfMatched = false;
            $anyOfErrors = [];
            foreach ($schema['anyOf'] as $branch) {
                if (!is_array($branch)) {
                    continue;
                }

                if (
                    array_key_exists('type', $branch)
                    && !($normalizedValue === null && ($branch['nullable'] ?? false) === true)
                    && !self::matchesOpenApiCallbackType($normalizedValue, $branch['type'])
                ) {
                    continue;
                }
                $errorsForBranch = self::validateOpenApiNode($value, $branch, $path, $depth + 1);
                if ($errorsForBranch === []) {
                    $anyOfMatched = true;
                    break;
                }

                $anyOfErrors = [...$anyOfErrors, ...$errorsForBranch];
            }

            if (!$anyOfMatched) {
                $errors = $anyOfErrors === []
                    ? [...$errors, self::describeOpenApiUnionMismatch($path, 'anyOf', $schema['anyOf'], $value)]
                    : [...$errors, ...array_values(array_unique($anyOfErrors))];
            }
        }

        return $errors;
    }

    /**
     * Whether a subschema matches; its errors are thrown away. The path is not: an unfinished check —
     * the depth limit, a pattern the engine gave up on — is thrown out of here with it, and is the
     * refusal the caller ends up reporting, so it has to name the value it could not finish.
     *
     * @param array<string, mixed> $schema
     */
    private static function matchesOpenApiNode(mixed $value, array $schema, string $path, int $depth): bool
    {
        return self::validateOpenApiNode($value, $schema, $path, $depth) === [];
    }

    /**
     * Every branch was gated out by its declared type, so no branch has a reason to report. Naming the
     * types the union does accept turns an unactionable sentence into one the caller can act on. Same
     * wording as the runtime validator, so a payload reads the same whichever mode answered it.
     *
     * @param array<int, mixed> $branches
     */
    private static function describeOpenApiUnionMismatch(string $path, string $kind, array $branches, mixed $value): string
    {
        $types = [];
        foreach ($branches as $branch) {
            if (!is_array($branch)) {
                continue;
            }

            $declared = is_array($branch['type'] ?? null) ? $branch['type'] : [$branch['type'] ?? null];
            foreach ($declared as $type) {
                if (is_string($type) && $type !== '' && !in_array($type, $types, true)) {
                    $types[] = $type;
                }
            }
        }

        if ($types === []) {
            return sprintf('%s does not match any %s branch', $path, $kind);
        }

        $last = array_pop($types);
        $expected = $types === [] ? $last : implode(', ', $types) . ' or ' . $last;

        return sprintf(
            '%s does not match any %s branch (expected %s, got %s)',
            $path,
            $kind,
            $expected,
            match (true) {
                $value === null => 'null',
                is_bool($value) => 'boolean',
                is_int($value) => 'integer',
                is_float($value) => 'number',
                is_string($value) => 'string',
                is_array($value) => array_is_list($value) ? 'array' : 'object',
                default => 'object',
            },
        );
    }

    private static function matchesOpenApiCallbackType(mixed $value, mixed $type): bool
    {
        if (!is_string($type) || $type === '') {
            return true;
        }

        return match ($type) {
            'string' => is_string($value),
            'object' => is_array($value) || is_object($value),
            'null' => $value === null,
            default => true,
        };
    }

    private static function normalizeOpenApiStructuralValue(mixed $value): mixed
    {
        if (is_array($value) || !is_object($value)) {
            return $value;
        }

        // Any other object: only its public state is visible, under PHP property names.
        return get_object_vars($value);
    }

    private static function openApiChildPath(string $path, string $propertyName): string
    {
        // The first level names the field the way the runtime validator's caller does, so both
        // modes report the same subject: `field "tags" must ...`.
        return $path === 'payload'
            ? sprintf('field "%s"', $propertyName)
            : $path . '.' . $propertyName;
    }
}
